# DECISIONS

Current product/engineering decisions. Section 17 (D01–D14) and 20.19 (WA-D01–WA-D15) of the master roadmap list owner decisions with proposed defaults; a proposed default applies until the owner records otherwise (roadmap §7). Entries here record **settled** choices and implementation decisions, newest first. Roadmap: `Road Map/Punjab_Learning_Portal_Master_Roadmap_v2.2_Integrated_Written_Assessment.md` (sha256 `84185cab…41b1`).

| ID | Date | Decision | Authority | Consequences |

| LECTURE-READ-01 | 2026-10-18 | **A topic's lecture reaches learners only when approved and servable (#237).** The reader control is English | Roman Urdu | Lecture. `GET /v1/topics/{id}/lecture` lists the topic's academically approved lecture parts (a storyboard pinned to the topic; parts in title order) whose registered master (file name and byte size) can be served; otherwise `coming`, and the lesson text stays. Watching follows the premium entitlement (plan or trial), and staff who can preview the subject may watch; a guest or unentitled learner sees the parts as locked with no stream. The stream (`GET /v1/lectures/{id}/stream`, range requests) reads `PORTAL_LECTURE_MEDIA_DIR` in the development and test roles only; deployed roles refuse the setting, because the media provider (B05) is the production path. Mobile plays with `expo-video` (native module: iOS build 4), the web through its own origin with the session cookie. **Development registration:** `content/tools/register_lectures.py` walks the portal's own lecture workflow (storyboard, captions, markers, master, submission, review) with the dev-seed staff accounts from the owner's production records; the reviewer fixture's approval is a technical test registration, named as such in the review notes, and is not academic approval. Chapter 1's 17 parts were registered that way on 18 October in the `portal` development database; the master files stay in the owner's folder and are never committed. **Roman Urdu glossing (owner, 18 October):** the first time a hard Urdu word appears in a topic it carries the English word in brackets; applied to all eight RU drafts. **2D first (owner, 18 October):** every figure starts as a 2D drawing; a 3D model only where a 2D drawing cannot convey the structure. | Owner, 18 October 2026 (phone review of build 3) | Media provider for production (B05); academic review of lectures and lessons before publication; renderer-2 client manifest (CLIENT-FLOOR-01); device walkthrough of build 4 |

| GIT-01 | 2026-10-07 | **Pushed history is never rewritten.** No force pushes of any kind, including `--force-with-lease`. Published branches are updated with new commits or merges. For stacked PRs, retarget dependent PRs to `main` (or merge them) **before** the base branch is deleted, and never use `--delete-branch` on a base that still has open dependents. *Record:* on 2026-10-07 `feat/mobile-shell` was force-pushed with lease twice (my own unshared branch, after rebases) and stacked PR #2 auto-closed when its base was deleted. Both practices are now prohibited. | Owner instruction | Merge `main` into feature branches instead of rebasing published ones. |
| RESCAN-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Learner actions on pending questions: readability rescans, reviewed classification, confirmed-unanswered and a 7-day deadline (W04.S3.T2, W06.S2.T4, §20.7.3, §20.6.3, §20.10; WA-AC62/63/82).** A teacher may leave a question pending and ask the learner to act: `rescan` (unreadable) or `confirm_or_rescan` (mapped evidence looks blank). The request carries one deadline (7 days from release); re-asking the same action never extends it. **Rescan:** an immutable `written_evidence_revision` beside the sealed original, holding hashes, the prior sealed pages, actor, note, time and `post_cutoff`. It is parsed and stored with no connection held, its files and pages have status `revision` (never part of the sealed manifest or caps), and limits are 3 copies per question, 3 pages each, and no byte-identical resend. **Classification:** the releasing reviewer must classify every unclassified rescan of the case's questions as READABILITY, NEW_CONTENT or INDETERMINATE with a reason, seeing original and copy side by side. Nothing is classified automatically; only READABILITY may be used to assess the answer. NEW_CONTENT leaves the original result and points the learner to a new practice test (the linked charged attempt of W04.S3.T3 is not built yet); INDETERMINATE keeps the question pending or unavailable. **Confirm unanswered:** zero under the rubric, maximum kept, and the question's unconsumed allowance released, the same as declaring it before sealing. It is published as a `LEARNER` version under the attempt lock and closes or rescopes the completion case. **Deadline:** `portal-written-learner-deadlines` (to run hourly) resolves questions still awaiting the learner, with no copy awaiting review, as unavailable with allowance returned (`SYSTEM` version, no assessor). Silence is never consent and never a zero. *Evidence:* `tests/test_rescans.py` (5); E2E journey (teacher asks, learner sends a copy, reviewer classifies READABILITY and marks, learner sees the outcome). The E2E also caught a workspace bug where typing a reason dropped the requested action (fixed). | Engineering (roadmap W04.S3.T2, W06.S2.T4) | Linked new practice attempt (W04.S3.T3); notifications for requests and deadlines (P15.S1); mobile learner screens. |
| RESCAN-02 | 2026-10-08 | **PR #31 review corrections (RS31-01/02/03) and evidence provenance (section 4).** Each finding was reproduced before it was fixed. **RS31-01 retries and races:** a rescan now needs an `Idempotency-Key` header (`KEY_REQUIRED` 422 without one), and `(attempt_id, idempotency_key)` is unique with a stored request fingerprint (position, file SHA-256 and note). Resending the same key and request returns the original acknowledgement (200, `replay: true`) and creates nothing, even after the deadline. The same key with a different request is 409 `KEY_REUSED`. Two identical files sent at the same moment give one 201 and one 409 `SAME_FILE`, never a 500: the key, action, limit and same-file checks are repeated under the attempt lock, and an `IntegrityError` is mapped by constraint name. Only this request's own objects are cleaned up. The learner web panel sends one key per send and retries once with the same key. **RS31-02 durable deadline:** the learner request is now a durable `written_learner_obligation` row (one open row per question, partial unique index). Its deadline is set once, on first release, and is never moved by a later save, a change of action or the action being left out. Confirming unanswered a second time does nothing. A rescan received on time marks the obligation as answered, so the deadline job never treats it as silence; that job also skips questions with a copy waiting for classification. **RS31-03 `post_cutoff`:** the flag is computed under the lock from the stored admission time against the attempt's `upload_cutoff_at` (`admitted_at > cutoff`), never defaulted. The audit record keeps `admitted_at`, `upload_cutoff_at`, `post_cutoff` and `post_seal`. `portal-written-repair` corrects wrong historical flags, with an audit record for each; it corrected **one** dev record. **Section 4 provenance:** a score version has `evidence_revisions` (position -> READABILITY revision id, file id and SHA-256), named by the deciding reviewer ("used to mark" in the workspace). Only READABILITY copies of the same attempt and question, and only for scored questions, are accepted (otherwise `EVIDENCE_NOT_ELIGIBLE`). Positions not named were marked from the sealed original, which stays allowed even when another copy is NEW_CONTENT or INDETERMINATE. Provenance carries forward through rechecks and completion publications for questions they don't change. Migration `74ef206e3de6` backfills keys (`legacy-<id>`) and obligations from the current released versions, keeping their deadlines; it round-trips. *Evidence:* `tests/test_rescan_corrections.py` (9: barrier race, replay/`KEY_REUSED`/after deadline/missing key, repeated confirmation, deadline unchanged by action changes or omission, timely rescan not silence, `post_cutoff` before and after U with matching audit, boundary, provenance with mixed revisions and recheck carry-forward, original evidence despite an INDETERMINATE copy); API suite 204 passed; E2E rescan journey with key and evidence selection. | Engineering (PR #31 review) | Native learner rescan screens must send the same key header; notifications (P15.S1). |
| LINKED-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Separate new learning attempts (W04.S3.T3; §20.7.3, §20.13.4; WA-AC24).** New or rewritten work is never marked as if it were the sealed original. The learner prepares a *linked* practice test: a new `written_form` carrying `linked_from_attempt_id`, `link_reason` (REWRITE, NEW_CONTENT or INDETERMINATE), `link_revision_id` and `link_positions`, holding only the chosen questions of the original. It is then started through the ordinary `start` path, so it has its own attempt, its own reservation and ledger identity, and its own review case and result. Preparing it reserves nothing; the confirmation page shows the weighted allowance units starting it will use and the learner's available units first. **Eligibility:** only the owner, and only for a sealed attempt. REWRITE needs a released result (before release the learner is pointed to a recheck, which re-marks the *same* evidence). NEW_CONTENT and INDETERMINATE need a learner rescan of that attempt and question with exactly that reviewer classification. **Version policy:** the linked test uses each question's *currently published* question and rubric versions, not the original's pinned ones, so a corrected question is practised in its corrected form; a question withdrawn since is refused (`QUESTION_WITHDRAWN`), and questions whose version changed are listed in the creation audit record. The original attempt keeps its own pinned versions. Preparing is idempotent by `Idempotency-Key` (same request returns the same test; a different request with the key is 409 `KEY_REUSED`). The one-attempt-per-form invariant is unchanged. **The original never changes:** its evidence, versions, history and allowance events are untouched; its result lists linked tests (`linked_attempts`), and a linked attempt shows `linked_from`. *Evidence:* `tests/test_linked_attempts.py` (3: rewrite before and after release, replay/`KEY_REUSED`, own reservation on start, linked attempt sealed and marked with the original result byte-for-byte unchanged; NEW_CONTENT only for the classified question and reason; owner/sealed/position checks); API suite passed; E2E (prepare, cost shown, start, linked notice, original marks unchanged). | Engineering (roadmap W04.S3.T3) | Native screens; notifications (P15.S1). Commerce qualification of allowance use remains WG08. |
| REVIEW-PR32 | 2026-10-08 | **PR #32 review corrections (PR32-01 to PR32-04).** Each was reproduced first by a failing test against real PostgreSQL (`tests/test_pr32_corrections.py`, 8 tests: 7 failed before the fix, 1 guard already held). **PR32-01 learner obligations:** the open `written_learner_obligation` is now the single authority for a pending question's request. A decision that keeps the question pending but omits the action carries the open request's action forward. Response admission (`_requested_action`), the learner result projection and the deadline job all read the obligation (`effective_request`), so control, admission and expiry agree. There is no silent withdrawal: only resolving the question (scored or unavailable) closes a request, so its first deadline can't be reset. When the deadline passes after a timely response that staff reviewed but couldn't use, the unavailable reason says the copies couldn't establish the answer, not that nothing arrived; the audit record lists those positions. Score versions are immutable, so no rows were rewritten: older versions that lost the action are corrected at read time from the obligation. **PR32-02 complete drafts:** a draft version stores `draft_intent` (proposed classifications and selected evidence) beside its statuses and awards. The case detail returns `draft` (in-scope non-scored statuses with reason and action, proposals, evidence) only when the latest version is this case's unreleased draft, and the workspace restores them. On a draft save, a proposal may stand in for an unclassified copy's class when checking evidence. It is never applied: no classification, audit, learner deadline, publication or allowance change. A draft no longer stores an invented action deadline. Release still classifies (audited) and then validates evidence against the applied class. **PR32-03 linked-form race:** reproduced as a `UniqueViolation` on `uq_written_form_idempotency` raised by the query-invoked autoflush of `db.get(ContentVersion)` (linked.py:132), outside the commit-only handler. All reads now happen before any row is added. The form is flushed inside the guarded block and the items and audit follow. Only that constraint (by name) is treated as a replay: same payload returns the winner, a different payload gives 409 `KEY_REUSED`, and anything else re-raises. **PR32-04:** clearer copies expose `post_cutoff`, `upload_cutoff_at` and `cutoff_timing` (before/at/after); the workspace shows that instead of the unconditional "after the upload deadline"; stale module wording corrected. *Evidence:* the 8 tests above (barrier-synchronised independent sessions for the race; one form, two items, one audit event, no reservation); E2E rescan journey now saves, navigates away and reopens both a pending draft (outcome, action, reason) and a classification/evidence draft, and checks the timing label. | Engineering (PR #32 review) | — |
| ADJ-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Rubric adjudications across attempts (W06.S2.T3; §20.10; WA-AC26/64/77/78).** A rubric correction is made through the editorial workflow (revise, review, publish), so new forms use it. Applying it to work already started or marked is a separate, audited decision. An academic adjudicator in the item's class and subject creates a `written_rubric_adjudication` naming the superseded rubric versions it corrects (default: every earlier published version for the same question version), the corrected published version and a reason (20+ characters). **Compatibility is computed, never supplied by the client:** a per-criterion diff over the fields that decide a mark (subpart, description, maximum, levels, dependencies, alternative group) plus rubric-level scoring rules. *Carry forward* only when all of these are identical, i.e. only reviewer notes such as expected concepts changed. **Compatible denominators:** every slot maximum must match (`INCOMPATIBLE_DENOMINATOR` otherwise; a different total is a question change). **Effective set:** active corrections chain (A→B, B→C) and the target is the end of the chain, so each run applies the complete approved set and jobs converge. A correction that claims a version already claimed by an active one must name it in `supersedes_id` (`SUPERSEDE_REQUIRED`), which marks the earlier one superseded; creation is serialised per rubric with an advisory lock. **Regrade job** (`POST …/adjudications/{id}/run`, `portal-written-regrade`): bounded batches, one short transaction per attempt (attempt row lock, then that attempt's queued cases), resumable and idempotent through a `written_rubric_regrade` row per (adjudication, attempt). Per affected question: *scored + identical scoring basis* → a new released version with identical marks re-based onto the corrected rubric, `decision_method=SYSTEM`, `case_kind=regrade`, with `regrade_detail` recording the carried human decisions and compatibility evidence (never labelled TEACHER). *Scored + changed basis* → never recomputed by machine; a queued `regrade` case asks a teacher to re-mark under the corrected rubric, and the released result stays until that teacher releases. If an open case (e.g. a recheck) already covers the question it is re-targeted instead, with its version bumped so a stale save conflicts. *Pending, unavailable or not yet marked* → re-targeted for later marking (`written_rubric_target`; `case_context` uses it). Regrade decisions may only score the case's questions, and publication still goes through the existing lease, expected-version and base-version checks. Each affected learner gets one deduplicated `written_notice` per correction, shown on the result; history labels the regrade. Nothing consumes or releases allowance; evidence, earlier versions and forms are never edited. *Evidence:* `tests/test_rubric_adjudication.py` (5, real PostgreSQL): carry-forward once with SYSTEM label and identical marks, idempotent re-run, adjudicator-only creation; changed basis goes to a teacher, the result is held, the case shows the corrected rubric, pending is refused, TEACHER release; retargeting of unmarked and pending questions through a chain, explicit supersede; two concurrent runs regrade each attempt once; an open recheck re-targeted rather than duplicated. Studio pages: list, create from the corrected rubric's page, impact preview, run next batch; seeded dev adjudicator `studio-adjudicator@example.com`; E2E smoke for adjudicator access. | Engineering (roadmap W06.S2.T3) | No browser journey for the full correction/regrade flow yet (API-tested); automatic (AI) regrades don't exist; notifications beyond the in-result notice (P15.S1); deployment must run `portal-written-regrade` or the studio button after each approved correction. |
| REVIEW-W06 | 2026-10-08 | **PR #34 review corrections for rubric adjudication (W06-01 to W06-10).** Before the fix, a temporary probe on the old code reproduced W06-01 (a non-MFA adjudicator's create returned 201), W06-02 (an active attempt got a rubric target) and W06-04 (an unrelated `CheckViolation` at commit was counted as `already_done` and left 1 remaining). **W06-01:** create, apply and retry use `require(Permission.adjudicate)`, so `MFA_REQUIRED` applies, and the service checks scope and MFA again. Reading (list, detail, sources, jobs, drill-down) stays open to scoped academic staff. Jobs record the requesting adjudicator, and each regraded script's audit names the job and the approving adjudicator. The old synchronous CLI `portal-written-regrade` is removed; the worker only runs jobs that an MFA adjudicator requested or that a seal queued under an approved correction. **W06-02 (policy):** only sealed attempts are regraded. A test still being written keeps its pinned rubric and gets no target or notice. Sealing queues any active correction for it in the same transaction, and marking a sealed script whose effective basis has moved but hasn't been applied is refused (`CORRECTION_PENDING`). There is no mid-attempt overlay; if academic policy later wants one, it needs its own versioned record. **W06-03:** every new score version stores `effective_adjudication` (position → authorised correction chain behind the rubric it was marked under; [] = pinned) and `adjudication_hash`. This applies to teacher decisions (initial, completion, recheck, regrade), SYSTEM carry-forward and learner/deadline resolutions. It is derived from the pinned basis through the active corrections, never from mutable state, and questions outside a decision's scope keep their earlier rubric id and provenance. A partial unique index `(attempt_id, adjudication_hash)` on SYSTEM regrade versions blocks a duplicate carry-forward. Versions released before this have null provenance. **W06-04:** only the `uq_written_regrade` and `uq_written_regrade_version` conflicts count as already done; any other error is re-raised, the script is recorded `failed` with its error and audited, the job ends `failed`, and an adjudicator can retry. **W06-05:** each regrade transaction takes the same per-rubric advisory lock as creation/supersession (lock first, then the attempt row) and re-checks status. The effective chain starts from the pinned rubric, so a superseding correction re-targets scripts the old one had moved. A supersede may name several active corrections (`supersedes_ids`, `superseded_by_id`) and must match the overlap exactly. A cycle or a chain longer than 20 steps raises `ChainError` (`CHAIN_INVALID` at creation). **W06-06:** applying queues a durable `written_regrade_job` (one open job per correction; repeating returns it). The separate worker process `portal-written-worker` (also started in CI's E2E job) claims jobs with SKIP LOCKED and a 2-minute lease, runs bounded batches (one short transaction per script, a fresh session per drain), and records processed, regraded, unaffected, failed, remaining and the last error. A crashed worker's lease expires and the job resumes from per-script checkpoints. The web page polls job status. **W06-07:** the studio form lists every earlier version with computed compatibility, a denominator check, the active corrections claiming it and its submitted-script count (`GET adjudication-sources`); the adjudicator picks exact source versions and ticks each correction being replaced. **W06-08:** the API returns total, processed, remaining, failed, unaffected and applied outcomes separately, and the UI shows each. **W06-09:** the preview uses a fixed number of aggregate queries (the same count for 1 or 4 affected scripts); the list view no longer computes impact; there is a paginated drill-down. Latency and memory at large history were **not** measured; that stays with production load evidence. **W06-10:** a Playwright journey: a dev-only command (`portal-dev-fixture-rubric-correction`, refused outside development/test) sets up an isolated question and publishes the corrected rubric, because dev can't publish through the rights gate. Then a learner submits, a teacher marks, a non-MFA adjudicator is refused, an MFA adjudicator selects the version, sees compatibility and impact, approves and applies, the worker finishes, re-applying processes nothing, a competing correction is refused until it names the one it replaces, the learner sees the notice and that question 1 awaits re-marking, an independent teacher re-marks under the corrected rubric, and the learner sees the new mark and history. **Also:** the adjudicator who approved a correction can't lease the regrade case it caused; the learner result adds `awaiting_regrade`, so wording doesn't claim complete teacher marking while a re-mark is pending; cutoff timing no longer relies on exact timestamp equality. *Evidence:* `tests/test_w06_review.py` (8) and the updated `tests/test_rubric_adjudication.py` (5); API suite 228 passed, exit 0; E2E 48 passed, 24 skipped, exit 0, including `adjudication.spec.ts`. | Engineering (PR #34 review) | Mid-attempt overlay policy (none; an explicit decision would be needed); large-history preview/regrade latency and memory; native screens. |
| NATIVE-W01 | 2026-10-08 | **Native written practice, results, rescans and help (W03/W04/W06 learner side; P15.S3 on mobile).** `apps/mobile` gains: a *Written answers* mode on the Practice tab (reviewed-question chapters only; refuses when no funded reviewer exists or marking is at capacity; one idempotency key per start); a written runner (`written/[id]`) that shows the questions, captures pages with `expo-image-picker` (camera with a permission prompt, or several photos from the library, requesting iOS `Compatible` representation so HEIC becomes JPEG, with a client-side type check before the server's validation), uploads each file as a raw body, maps pages to every answer slot or 'I didn't answer this' with expected-revision saves (a conflict reloads), and submits once with a stable key for a receipt; the result view (teacher marks, per-criterion reasons, honest completeness, marking-guide notices, `awaiting_regrade`, history, a recheck request on the same evidence); pending learner actions (a clearer copy with one `Idempotency-Key` per chosen photo and one retry with the same key, or 'I didn't answer this'); and a Help screen (open a request, including a question report linked to a written result, list requests and reply). The picker config plugin sets photo and camera permission text and disables microphone permission. *Evidence:* mobile `tsc`, `expo lint` and `expo-doctor` (21/21) are clean. The journey was exercised on **Expo web only** in the browser pane: start → upload (the web file chooser stubbed in-page with a generated PNG) → map, including a declared-unanswered part → submit with receipt → teacher pending/rescan request (released through the dev API) → clearer copy accepted → question report sent → completion released → marks, history and recheck requested. **Not verified on Android or iOS devices** (camera, HEIC transcoding, file URIs, background/kill behaviour); that remains B07/B08/B13. There is no automated Expo-web E2E in CI yet. | Engineering (roadmap W03/W04/W06, P15.S3) | Real-device verification; an automated mobile E2E harness; offline capture queue (photos kept on device until uploaded); PDF selection on native (document picker); linked practice and recheck notifications. |
| REVIEW-PR36 | 2026-10-08 | **PR #36/#38 review corrections (NEW-11 to NEW-15) and smaller gaps.** Each was first reproduced by a failing test against real PostgreSQL (`tests/test_pr36_review.py` and `test_recheck.py`): a non-MFA rebase and widening returned 200; after a crash between per-script commits and the counter update the job ended `succeeded` with `processed 0` (and a failed script resumed as `succeeded`); the open regrade case kept the superseded correction's id; replacing an ancestor with an active descendant was accepted. **NEW-11:** rebasing a recheck goes through `require(Permission.adjudicate)` (MFA_REQUIRED). The service (`require_adjudication`) checks permission and MFA for rebase and for widening a recheck, and `can_expand` is shown only with MFA. Existing expansion tests that used non-MFA adjudicators were changed to MFA (they encoded the gap). **NEW-12:** job counters (processed, regraded, unaffected, failed, last error) are recomputed from the durable per-script rows the job wrote, filtered by `job_id` and correction, on every batch, including a resume after a crash. A failure row on retry keeps its job id. A failed script can't become success. **NEW-13:** when a newer correction reaches a question that an open regrade case covers, the case is re-pointed to it and the old identity is audited (`written.regrade_case_retargeted`). Independence is checked against every approver in the effective chain of the case's questions (`chain_approvers`), and teacher-release provenance names the effective correction. **NEW-14 (policy):** superseding a correction must resolve each active descendant (corrections from the version it led to, transitively): replace it too, by covering its source versions, or keep it explicitly (`retain_descendant_ids`, stored as `retained_descendant_ids`). Otherwise the request gets 409 `DESCENDANTS_UNRESOLVED` listing them. A kept descendant goes on serving scripts pinned on its own source version, while scripts on the replaced versions follow the replacement. Effective chains always start from the pinned rubric. This is enforced under the per-rubric lock. **NEW-15:** a run applies the complete chain and records a `done` row for every correction in it (`applied_via` = the correction whose run applied it), so whichever job runs first the rows, counts and the single notice are the same and the other job finds nothing to do. Sealing queues the chain ancestors first, with distinct `clock_timestamp()` creation times. **Smaller gaps:** the correction list applies scope in SQL before `offset/limit`; the preview counts clearer copies only on the affected questions (`count(distinct)`); each replace/keep option shows the correction's versions, approval time and reason. Script totals were already distinct per attempt (`select distinct`), and one rubric can't appear twice in a form, so per-version counts can't double count. **Local benchmark** (opt-in `PORTAL_BENCH=120 pytest tests/test_bench_regrade.py`, development laptop, disposable database): 120 teacher-marked scripts, preview p50 65 ms / max 75 ms with 10 SQL statements per request, worker regrade 120 scripts in 19.5 s (6.1/s, carry-forward path). These are development numbers, not production capacity evidence (B03/P20). *Evidence:* API suite 235 passed, exit 0; E2E 48 passed, 24 skipped, exit 0. | Engineering (PR #36/#38 review) | Production load/large-history evidence (B03); device verification (B14). |
| NOTIF-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Notification foundation (P15.S1.T1–T3).** **Events and templates** (`notifications/templates.py`): account verification, payment receipt, trial ending, written result released, written action required, score revised, support reply, mock and revision reminders, app update required and new content. Each template has a category (`service` / `reminder` / `promotional`), its channels, an urgency flag and required parameters (rendering refuses missing ones). Texts never include marks, keys or tokens. Service notices always reach the inbox; reminders can be switched off; promotional notices are recorded only after an explicit opt-in (default off). **Emitting:** `notify()` runs inside the caller's transaction and is idempotent per (user, dedupe key). It is wired into: teacher releases (result released, plus each requested action with its deadline in the learner's timezone), SYSTEM deadline resolutions, rubric-correction notices (score revised), staff support replies (internal notes never notify) and a daily `portal-notification-trial-reminders` job (3 days before a trial ends, once per trial). Payment, mock, revision, update-required and verification emitters land with their features (B04/B06 and later phases); their templates already exist. **Delivery** (`portal-notification-worker`): the in-app inbox needs none. Email and push deliveries are queued per channel (unique per notification), claimed with SKIP LOCKED, and wait out quiet hours and a cap of 10 non-urgent messages per user, channel and hour; urgent notices skip both. Failures retry with exponential backoff and stop as `failed` after 5 attempts. Email uses `dev_outbox` in development/test (refused in staging/production by the configuration validator) or `none`, which records deliveries as `unavailable` until an approved sender exists (B04). Push records `skipped` without a registered device and `unavailable` with one, until builds and push credentials exist (B07). Device tokens can already be registered and removed. SMS has no adapter (optional, approved-cost channel). **Preferences:** email, push, reminders, promotional opt-in, IANA timezone (default `Asia/Karachi`; validated) and quiet hours in local HH:MM, which may cross midnight. Everything is stored in UTC. **Clients:** web `/notifications` (unread count, open marks read and follows only internal app paths, mark all read; times in the learner's timezone), reached from `/account` (no extra header link: the phone header is full), and preferences on `/account`. Mobile has a Notifications screen (open → read → the matching native screen) linked from Account. *Evidence:* `tests/test_notifications.py` (10, real PostgreSQL) passed locally before the local Docker engine failed; the full API suite and E2E (including the new inbox step in the marking journey) run in CI on this PR's head. | Engineering (roadmap P15.S1) | Email sender (B04); native push builds/credentials (B07); emitters for payments (B06), mocks and revision plans when those features exist. |
| HELP-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Help centre, contextual guidance and incident notices (P15.S2.T1–T3).** **Articles:** `help_article` (slug × locale `en`/`ur`) with immutable `help_article_version`s. They are written in a small Markdown subset (paragraphs, `#`/`##` headings, `-`/`1.` lists, `>` notes) and converted to the validated content blocks lessons use, so web and native render them with the existing renderers. Saving identical text creates nothing. Staff with the new `manage_help` permission (support role) draft; publishing and retiring also need an MFA session. Readers see only published versions; full-text search (PostgreSQL `simple` configuration) covers published text; a missing Urdu version falls back to English with a flag. **Starting articles:** nine English articles in `apps/api/help_articles/en/` describe only implemented behaviour and its real limits: upload limits and photo quality (JPEG/PNG 15 MB, PDF 40 MB/10 pages, 10 pages/10 files/60 MB, 800 px warning), submission and receipts (10-minute upload after timed writing, 24-hour untimed permit, idempotent submit), marking (48-hour due time, pending/unavailable never zero, practice marks aren't official results), clearer copies (7 days, the three outcomes), rechecks (14 days, independent teacher, no extra allowance), allowance units, the free trial, practice saving and the 3-second timer grace, and signing in/devices. `portal-help-import` loads them as **drafts only**; staff review and publish. No article claims unbuilt features (payments, video, offline study) or unproven ones (trial repeat-protection across accounts). Urdu translations need a reviewed translator (not invented). **Contextual guidance:** help links from upload rejections, an expired written test, a teacher's clearer-copy request and the offline saving state; the error page offers 'Copy details for support' (page, time, browser and the error reference only; never tokens, cookies, answers or keys). **Incidents:** `service_incident` with a public timeline; a platform operator with MFA opens and updates them. `GET /v1/status` is public; a banner above every page shows unresolved incidents and never replaces the page content, so receipts and results stay visible. A status page hosted apart from the app needs separate hosting (B03). **Native:** the Help screen searches published articles and opens them (`help-article/[slug]`). *Evidence:* `tests/test_help.py` (the converter and all nine bundled articles validate locally; the database flows run in CI because local Docker is down); E2E: support staff (MFA) draft → hidden → publish → a visitor finds it by search. | Engineering (roadmap P15.S2) | Urdu translations (reviewed translator); independent status hosting (B03); help links for payments when B06 lands. |
| SUPPORT-02 | 2026-10-09 | **Delivery reliability, support abuse limits, escalation and resolution notices (P15.S4.T1/T2, P15.S3.T2).** **Delivery failures:** failed deliveries (after 5 attempts with backoff) form a dead-letter list operators (`operate_platform`, MFA) can inspect and requeue (audited; only failed ones). Destinations can be suppressed (bounces, complaints, requests); only a SHA-256 of the normalised address is stored, the email adapter honours it, and suppressing/unsuppressing is audited. `GET /v1/ops/notifications/metrics` reports delivery outcomes per channel separately from inbox created/read counts. Automatic bounce suppression needs a real sender (B04). **Abuse limits:** at most 10 new requests a day and 20 learner messages an hour per account (429 `RATE_LIMITED`), on top of the existing 5-open limit. There is no student-to-student channel, so no contact exchange can happen between learners. Attachments land with P15.S3.T1 screenshots. **Escalation:** support staff escalate a request with a reason (≥10 characters, once, audited). Escalated requests are listed first and badged; the reason is a staff note the learner never sees. **Resolution notice:** resolving a request (including an academic report) sends one `support.resolved` notice instead of a reply notice; internal notes never notify. *Evidence:* `tests/test_support_reliability.py` (5) in CI (local Docker down). | Engineering (roadmap P15.S4, P15.S3.T2) | Bounce/complaint webhooks with the email provider (B04). |
| SUPPORT-03 | 2026-10-09 | **Screenshots on help requests (P15.S3.T1).** A learner can add up to 5 screenshots to an open request (10 a day per account; PNG or JPEG only, at most 5 MB; PDFs refused). Uploads are quarantined by construction: they are decoded in the same isolated evidence worker as written scripts, and only its re-encoded PNG (no metadata) is stored in the private store under `support/{ticket}/`. The original bytes are never kept; only their SHA-256 is recorded. The row is written after the object is stored; a failure discards the object. Screenshots are served with `private, no-store`, `nosniff` and inline disposition to the request's owner and to staff who can see the request (support staff; subject reviewers only for in-scope academic reports). The evidence orphan sweep treats them as referenced. Web: a same-origin upload route (Origin must match Host) and an image route under `/help/{id}/screenshots`; thumbnails on the learner and staff pages. Mobile has no screenshot picker yet (provisional: web only). Retention follows the request (no separate deletion yet; provisional until the privacy retention schedule, P16). *Evidence:* `tests/test_support_attachments.py` (3) and E2E `support.spec.ts` in CI (local Docker down). | Engineering (roadmap P15.S3.T1) | Mobile picker; retention schedule (P16). |
| SUPPORT-04 | 2026-10-09 | **Support lookup, redacted timeline and assisted access (P15.S3.T3).** A new `look_up_learners` permission (support role; MFA required) covers three things. **Lookup:** staff find a learner by exact, case-insensitive email, sent in a POST body and never in a URL. There are no partial matches or browsing, and staff accounts are never returned. Each lookup is audited with whether it found someone, but not the searched address. **Timeline:** an audited, newest-first list (at most 300 entries) of the account, trial grants, trial-claim transitions with their reasons (the trial decisions), device approvals and removals, trial exceptions, access sources and revocations, practice and written attempt start/finish, help requests (category and status only) and account actions on the learner. It never shows answers, uploads, message text, installation identifiers or provider evidence; staff lookups themselves are excluded. **Assisted access:** explicit, read-only and time-limited (5–30 minutes, default 15). It is tied to one of the learner's open requests, needs a reason of at least 10 characters that the learner sees, and allows one active grant per staff member per learner. It adds result summaries (latest MCQ score; written file counts) to that staff member's timeline only. The learner gets a `support.assisted_access` service notice, sees grants on their account page and can end them; staff can end their own. Grant, end and every view are audited. It never lets staff act as the learner. *Provisional:* learner consent is implied by an open request rather than asked separately; assisted access unlocks summaries only. *Evidence:* `tests/test_support_lookup.py` (3) and E2E in CI (local Docker down). | Engineering (roadmap P15.S3.T3) | Whether assisted access should need the learner's explicit in-app consent (owner/privacy decision; reversible). |

Each rehearsable incident has a test that walks the same steps through the API: `tests/test_incident_rehearsals.py`. **Missing paid access:** support looks the learner up and checks the timeline, then escalates; finance grants interim `promotional` access (≤ 7 days) whose reason cites the request; support resolves. This is provisional until payments exist (B06), when reconciling the payment comes first. **Trial false-block appeal:** the rehearsal found a defect. A reviewer's exception (§16.7) was consulted only when authorising a device for an *existing* grant, so a brand-new account on a second-hand phone stayed refused. Fix: a claim whose device evidence is consumed now proceeds when an active exception exists for that account and surface. The decision trail names the exception, the grant's device is recorded with method `exception`, and the exception's `used_at` is set. The device marker is never cleared. **Disputed question:** soft quarantine is rehearsed by existing tests; VOID/KEY_ERROR score propagation (§5.7, P10.S3.T4) is not built and is the next task. **Interrupted mock:** not rehearsable until timed mocks exist (P11). *Evidence:* the failing test in CI before the fix, then green. | Engineering (roadmap P15.S4.T3) | Payment reconciliation step once B06 lands. |
| MCQADJ-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Reviewed MCQ score corrections and score-version propagation (§5.7, P10.S3.T4, P08.S4.T1).** Corrections are a separate `mcq_adjudication` record for one exact published question version. They never edit the question, its key or any attempt. Academic adjudicators in the item's class and subject (MFA) record them only while the question is quarantined at the matching level. **VOID:** each affected form applies its own pinned `invalid_item_treatment`; EXCLUDE is the practice default, removing the item's earned marks (including penalties) and its maximum. **KEY_ERROR:** KEY_CORRECTION against a reviewed option, which must differ from the published key. There is one effective correction per version; a new one supersedes the earlier one explicitly, and an identical repeat is refused. **Propagation:** each finalised attempt containing the version is re-scored from its final-answer ledger with the complete effective set, one short transaction per attempt under the attempt lock. ScoreVersion n+1 is written only when the effective set changes; uniqueness on (attempt, policy, effective hash) makes reruns and duplicate requests no-ops. `portal-mcq-regrade` re-runs propagation after an interruption. Attempts open when a correction lands get it at submission. Learners get one `score.revised` notice per new version; the result shows the corrected key, per-item treatment and a revision note, and earlier versions are kept. **Quarantine levels:** a quarantined question's level can be confirmed or re-classified directly (SOFT to VOID, KEY_ERROR to VOID) without returning it to learners. Returning to SOFT, or releasing, is refused while a correction is effective; publish a corrected version instead. *Provisional/limits:* returning to an effective set that an attempt already had (a supersession back to an earlier correction) is logged and not re-scored, because uniqueness keeps one version per set; replacing unstarted affected forms with successor forms (§5.7 before start) is not built; written questions follow §20. *Evidence:* `tests/test_mcq_corrections.py` (3) in CI (local Docker down). | Engineering (roadmap §5.7, P10.S3.T4) | Successor forms for unstarted affected forms; mobile result revision note. |
| IMPORT-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Structured content import with preview and atomic commit (P06.S2.T1/T2).** Content authors import in batches from **Studio → Import** or `POST /v1/studio/imports`. Formats: JSON `schema_version: 1` for `mcq`, `lesson` and `written` (each kind's own body schema) and a CSV template for MCQs (plain paragraphs; unknown columns rejected); documented in `docs/content-import.md`. **Preview** is a dry run: every row is validated (chapter by stable key or ID, topic, scope, body schema, source references, publication-readiness warnings) and assigned an action: create, update (the external ID's item is still a draft and the content differs), unchanged (same payload hash), skip (in review, approved or published; revise in the studio) or error. Duplicate external IDs are errors; identical text is a warning. A CSV correction report is downloadable. **Commit** is refused while any row has an error. It re-validates every row against the current database and writes all of them in one transaction or none; a row that changed since the preview means preview again. Imports only create or update **drafts** (never submit, approve or publish) and are audited per item and per batch. **Idempotency:** `external_ref` is unique per kind with a payload hash; re-importing a file writes nothing; committing twice is refused; two racing batches can't both create one external ID. Limits: 5 MB, 2,000 rows, UTF-8, previews expire after 24 h. *Provisional:* no XLSX (save as CSV UTF-8 instead, which avoids a spreadsheet parser in the API); rubrics, media manifests and W01 script/label manifests are not imported here. *Evidence:* `tests/test_content_import.py` (3) and the studio E2E in CI. | Engineering (roadmap P06.S2) | XLSX support if the owner needs it; media manifests with P07. |
| ROLLBACK-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Release rollback per item (P06.S3.T3).** Publishers in scope (MFA) can put the previously published version of an item back in front of learners with a reason (`POST /v1/studio/items/{id}/rollback`, **Roll back to the previous version**). The current version becomes superseded and stays in the history; nothing is erased. The restored version must pass today's publication gate (rights, renderer and links). Rollback is refused if that version has an effective MCQ score correction, and it resolves any quarantine. Attempts keep their pinned versions; unstarted tests built on the rolled-back version do not start (AFFECTED-01). Audited as `content.rolled_back` with the from/to versions. *Provisional:* rollback is per item; catalogue-wide release bundles are part of P06.S3.T2. | Engineering (roadmap P06.S3.T3) | Release bundles. |
| EXPORT-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Portable content export (P06.S4.T2).** `GET /v1/studio/export?grade=&subject=` returns one JSON document per class and subject: the book (source ID, rights status), chapters and topics with stable keys, and every item in scope with all versions (body, source references, status, publication time) and MCQ score corrections. It is documented in `docs/content-import.md`, and an item can be re-imported as a draft. Only publishers in that scope with MFA can export. Every export is audited with its item count and byte size. Learner data, attempts, staff identities and notes are never included. *Provisional:* no media files (P07). | Engineering (roadmap P06.S4.T2) | Media in exports with P07. |
| AFFECTED-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Affected-attempt report and the §5.7 before-start rule (P08.S4.T2).** `GET /v1/studio/items/{id}/affected` gives, for each version of a question, the number of practice tests not yet started, attempts active and attempts released. It returns counts only, and is available to staff in scope (reviewers, publishers, quarantiners, adjudicators). An unstarted practice test whose question version is no longer the live published one (quarantined, retired, corrected, replaced or rolled back) does not start: 409 `FORM_SUPERSEDED`, and the learner builds a new test from approved versions. The shared original form is never changed. Started attempts keep their contents, and corrections reach them through MCQADJ-01. | Engineering (roadmap P08.S4.T2, §5.7) | Written forms (§20). |
| AUTOASSESS-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Automatic written assessment: contracts and job integrity, disabled (W05.S2, W05.S3; B10).** The adapter is set by `PORTAL_WRITTEN_ASSESSOR`: `none` by default, which records nothing and sends nothing; `fixture` is a deterministic technical fixture refused in staging and production. **Request:** the pinned question, the effective rubric (approved corrections win), and references to the sealed evidence (slot pages and the preview hashes teachers mark from). Student evidence is flagged untrusted, and no learner identity is ever included. **Output:** a strict schema (contract v1) with criterion awards in integer hundredths, reasons, evidence anchors, alternatives relied on and unresolved flags. It is validated with the same rules as teacher marking (permitted levels, unanswered slots, dependencies, one credited route per alternative group) plus the question maximum. Anchors outside the question's sealed pages, or regions off the page, are rejected. Any unresolved flag (unreadable, blank, missing continuation, unlisted method, diagram, conflicting readings) sends the question to review, never to an inferred mark. **Jobs:** one stage record per (receipt, question, rubric version, assessor), so duplicates are no-ops. Claims are leased with SKIP LOCKED, and no DB connection is held while the provider runs. Up to 3 attempts with backoff, then a dead letter that operators can requeue (audited). Unavailable is a visible status. Reconciliation enqueues sealed scripts that are missing rows. If the rubric or sealed evidence changes, the record is rejected for reassessment. Provenance records the assessor and version, contract version, request and output hashes, and latency. **Publication:** none. *Provisional and safest reversible default:* proposals are stored but never become results, so teacher marking stays the only route to a result. A fenced AI writer that can never replace a teacher, recheck or rescan result needs the WG10 reliability gate first. The reading adapter (W05.S1) and calibration need a qualified provider and approved data (B10). | Engineering (roadmap W05) | B10 provider/data approval; WG10 evidence before any publication path. |
| AUTHZ-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Route-level authorisation matrix (P18.S1.T2).** `tests/test_authorization_matrix.py` walks every API route from the application itself, so new routes are covered automatically. **Unauthenticated surface:** the set of routes reachable without a principal is pinned to a reviewed allowlist, with a reason for each of the 14 routes. A new public route fails CI until someone deliberately adds it. **Anonymous calls:** every other route, called anonymously with any method, placeholder IDs and an empty body, must answer 401. **Staff routes:** a signed-in learner, even with an MFA session, must never get a success or a server error from `/v1/studio`, `/v1/staff`, `/v1/ops` or `/v1/admin`. The first CI run (37917298599) found no gaps. This is not the independent security review (P18.S4); object-level student A/B checks stay in each module's tests. | Engineering (roadmap P18.S1.T2) | Revoked-user sweep; independent review. |
| STORYBOARD-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Video storyboards and prompt packages (P07.S4.T1/T2).** Storyboards are a new content kind on the same editorial workflow: scoped drafting, independent review against the checklist (accuracy, sources, pacing, accessibility, rights), the rights gate and publication. They are **never learner-readable**; learners only ever get the reviewed video. **Structure:** each scene has a stable ID and a contiguous time window, narration, on-screen text, equations, visual, transition, assets, accessibility notes, and the factual claims it teaches. Every claim must cite one of the version's source references. **Validation:** submission and publication both need a complete, continuous storyboard with cited claims and accessibility notes for visuals and equations; drafts may be incomplete. **Prompt package:** `GET /v1/studio/items/{id}/prompt-package` (staff in scope) exports the working or published version. Teaching constraints (fixed by review) are kept apart from creative direction, with resolved source citations, and each export is audited with its SHA-256. No AI is called by the portal, and generated media is never published from generation success (P07.S4.T3). *Provisional:* the media pipeline and the model evaluation contract (P07.S4.T4) wait on B05 and B01. | Engineering (roadmap P07.S4) | Media provider (B05); reviewers for the evaluation set (B01). |
| OPS-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Alert signals and feature switches (P17.S3.T1, P17.S3.T3).** `GET /v1/ops/signals` (platform operators, MFA) reports each watched value with warn and alert thresholds, an owner and a runbook anchor in `docs/runbooks/alerts.md`. The values: notification queue age and dead letters, written-regrade queue age and failed jobs, automatic-assessment dead letters, overdue and oldest marking cases, oldest open and escalated support requests, and this process's pool use. A deployment's monitoring polls it; paging and dashboards need hosting (B03). **Feature switches:** operators turn optional features off or on with a reason (≥10 characters, audited) through `PUT /v1/ops/features/{key}`. The switchable features are imports, exports, prompt packages, support screenshots, new practice tests and new written tests. Off means **new** work gets 503 `FEATURE_DISABLED`. Active attempts, saves, submissions, marking, results and entitlements are never behind a switch, so disabling a broken feature never interrupts an exam or loses work. Every feature is on unless switched off. *Provisional:* thresholds are engineering defaults to be tuned with real traffic. | Engineering (roadmap P17.S3) | Threshold tuning after launch traffic; paging integration (B03). |
| PRIVACY-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Data access and deletion requests (P18.S3.T2).** **Export:** a signed-in person downloads their own data as JSON from the account page (`GET /v1/me/data-export`, audited). It covers: account, profile, consents, sessions, trial grants, claims, devices and exceptions, access sources, the allowance ledger, practice attempts with answers and scores, written attempts with released results only, help requests and their non-internal messages, screenshots metadata, assisted access, notifications and preferences. It never includes password or token hashes, installation references, provider evidence, storage keys, staff identifiers (`*_by`, author or assessor), staff-only notes or escalation reasons, or anyone else's data. **Deletion:** confirmed by typing the account email; one open request at a time. It is recorded as an `account` help request with an audit entry so support can act. **Nothing is erased or anonymised automatically**, because what must be deleted, anonymised or retained needs the approved privacy and retention schedule (BLOCKERS B15). Payload values under sensitive keys (email, token, hash, password, secret, destination, IP, phone) are redacted, and every search is itself audited. | HEADERS-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Security headers (P18.S2.T1).** Neither the web app nor the API sent any before. **Web** (`next.config.ts`): Next's documented no-nonce Content-Security-Policy, because Cache Components and partial prefetching are incompatible with nonces. It sets `default-src 'self'`; scripts and styles from `'self'` plus `'unsafe-inline'` (`'unsafe-eval'` only in development); images from `'self'`, blob, data and https (the media origin is runtime configuration); `connect-src 'self'` (the browser only calls this origin); `object-src 'none'`; `base-uri` and `form-action 'self'`; and `frame-ancestors 'none'`. It also sends nosniff, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and a Permissions-Policy (camera self only; no microphone, geolocation or payment). **API:** nosniff, DENY, `no-referrer` and `default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, except the development-only `/docs`. `upgrade-insecure-requests` and HSTS are left to the TLS edge (B03), because plain-http localhost must work. *Provisional:* a stricter `script-src` without `'unsafe-inline'` needs a nonce or SRI rendering decision. | Engineering (roadmap P18.S2.T1) | Edge TLS/HSTS (B03). |

The automatic assessor gets pages only by preview hash, with the evidence marked untrusted and an instruction to ignore instructions inside it. No defect was found. **Not claimed:** prompt-injection resistance of a real model, which can only be evaluated once a provider is approved (B10); semantic hallucinations can't all be detected mechanically. *Evidence:* passed locally; full suite in CI. | Engineering (roadmap W08.S2.T2) | B10. |
| REGION-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Future-region isolation (P05.S4.T2).** *Defect found:* the catalogue model has regions, but nothing filtered by region. With a second region's records present, the catalogue listed every region's classes (two "Class XI"), and the book, chapter, lesson, search, pack and test-building queries picked classes by number alone. The catalogue response also hard-coded `pk-punjab`. *Fix:* a `released_regions` setting (default `["pk-punjab"]`; another region is added only when the owner authorises its release) and `curriculum/regions.py`, applied to: the catalogue; the book for a class and subject; book and chapter by id; lessons by chapter; search (topics, chapters, lessons); offline packs and their status; the practice, mock and pattern chapter queries; and written test forms. Studio tools are deliberately not filtered, so staff can prepare a region before release. *Evidence:* `tests/test_region_isolation.py` commits a labelled technical second region (one Class XI Physics book, chapter and topic) only for the test and removes it; no other province's academic catalogue is created. It failed before the fix (the catalogue listed `[11, 11, 12]`) and passes after, with the catalogue, search, practice-scope, profile-change, study-pack, written-attempt, lesson-access and authorization tests passing locally; full suite in CI. | Engineering (roadmap P05.S4.T2) | Owner: release of any new region. |
| RUBRIC-PATHS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Bounded criteria: unreachable credit paths (W01.S2.T1).** *Gap found:* a rubric could be submitted with a dependency cycle (A needs B, B needs A: no award order satisfies it) or with a criterion depending on another route of its own alternative group (only one route of a group is credited, so it could never earn credit). Only self-dependency and unknown criteria were caught. *Fix:* `validate_rubric` now names each cycle and each such dependency, and the rubric can't be submitted. The rest of W01.S2.T1 was already in place: stable criterion IDs, permitted levels from 0 to the maximum as multiples of the increment (so no negative deductions), dependencies on known criteria, and reconciliation of totals with the question and subparts (IMPL-19). Duplicated credit across alternative routes is capped by alternative groups. **Not detected:** two differently named criteria that describe the same credit; that is left to the reviewer's checklist. *Evidence:* two new cases in `tests/test_written_records.py::test_rubric_rules_and_reconciliation` (failed before the fix, pass after) with the written records, marking, automatic and rubric-adjudication tests passing locally; full suite in CI. | Engineering (roadmap W01.S2.T1) | None. |
| AUTO-RECOVERY-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Automatic-assessment retries and reconciliation (W05.S3.T1).** The stage records, backoff, dead letter and requeue were already tested; reconciliation and stale-lease reclaim existed but weren't. `tests/test_written_auto_recovery.py` now shows two things. First, a sealed script that never got stage records (a crash between sealing and enqueueing, or a newly enabled assessor) is enqueued once by `reconcile`, and running it again adds nothing. Second, a worker that dies holding a lease loses it on expiry: another worker reclaims the same record (attempt 2) and finishes it, and the dead worker's late result can't replace that outcome. No defect was found. Provider-side idempotency waits on a real provider (B10). *Evidence:* passed locally with the fixture assessor; full suite in CI. | Engineering (roadmap W05.S3.T1) | B10. |
| COMPLETENESS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Completeness states, including nothing assessable (W02.S1.T3, roadmap §20.9.3).** *Defect found:* a written paper where no question could be assessed (S = 0, P = 0, U = M) was released as `partial_unavailable` with a total of 0, which reads as a zero mark. *Fix:* `review.completeness_of` applies §20.9.3: pending wins whether or not some are unavailable (PARTIAL_PENDING, as before); nothing assessable is `unavailable`. The result then carries no total, the release notice says that none of the questions could be assessed and the allowance was returned, and web and mobile show "No mark for this test" instead of a partial score. **Compatibility (2026-10-13):** the first version sent `completeness: "unavailable"` and a null `history[].total_units`, which the oldest supported mobile app has never seen; the MIGRATION-COMPAT-01 check failed in CI (run 38309725305). The check was not changed. Instead, the legacy fields keep their old contract: `completeness` sends `partial_unavailable` for a paper with nothing assessable (the old app already shows "Some questions couldn't be assessed", no total, nothing counted as zero), and `history` lists only versions that have a mark, so the old app never prints a 0 for one that doesn't. Two additive fields carry the full state for current clients: `assessment_state` (complete, partial_pending, partial_unavailable, unavailable) and `versions` (every released version, with a null total when it has no mark). Web and mobile read the new fields. NOT_SCORABLE (M = 0) can't arise for a written paper, because every question has a positive maximum. The analytics `completeness` property documents the new value. The rest of W02.S1.T3 (weighted allowance ledger, permits, retries, separate admission, processing, resolution and provenance states) was already in place (ALLOC-01, ADMIT-01, IMPL-22, AUTOASSESS-01). *Evidence:* `tests/test_question_outcomes.py::test_a_paper_with_nothing_assessable_is_unavailable_not_partial` (failed before the fix; now also asserts the legacy values and the history split), `::test_pending_and_unavailable_together_are_pending_then_partial`, `::test_older_apps_only_get_completeness_values_they_know`, the oldest-mobile-client contract check, and `apps/web/e2e/written-results.spec.ts` (a teacher marks every question unavailable; the learner sees no mark and the reason; desktop, run locally) with the question-outcome, marking, signal, recheck, rescan, next-step and permit tests passing locally; full suite in CI; web and mobile typecheck and lint. | Engineering (roadmap W02.S1.T3) | None. |
| AUTO-PROVENANCE-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Assessor provenance and controlled failure (W05.S3.T2, W05.S3.T3).** *Gaps found:* provenance had no configuration hash and no cost or usage, there was no way to tell which model actually answered, and nothing tested an unexpected model change or that failures leave MCQ work alone. *Fix:* the assessor output contract takes optional `served_by` (the model version that answered) and `usage` (provider-reported units). Provenance records the configured assessor and version, `served_by` (null means the provider didn't say: a declared limitation), a configuration hash (assessor, version, contract, instructions), request and output hashes, latency and usage. Usage is priced later with the ECONOMICS-01 rates. If a model other than the qualified version answers, the record is rejected, proposes nothing, and keeps what happened. Already in place: no AI publication path exists, so a candidate can never replace a teacher, appeal or rescan result (AUTOASSESS-01); malformed, duplicate, timeout and unavailable outputs (existing tests). *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_auto_provenance.py`: provenance recorded with one configuration hash per run; a changed model is rejected; a provider failing every call is retried or dead-lettered without a mark while an MCQ practice test is submitted and scored. Passed locally with the automatic and recovery tests; full suite in CI. **Not claimed:** any real provider's behaviour (B10). | Engineering (roadmap W05.S3.T2-T3) | B10. |
| RELEASE-CANDIDATE-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Freezing a tested release candidate (P19.S4.T3).** *Gap found:* the RELEASE-01 manifest pinned the commit, migration head, contract, catalogue, lockfiles, renderer, analytics events and default switches, but not the native app identity, the published content set, the live switch and rollout state, or the build's known limitations. *Fix:* `python -m portal_api.release manifest` now adds `mobile_app` (the version and the development, preview and production application IDs, read from `apps/mobile/app.config.ts`, so a candidate can't silently ship a non-production ID); `content_release_id`, a SHA-256 over every live content item and its published version, plus `runtime_overrides` (the operators' switch and rollout rows), both read with a 3-second connection timeout when a database is reachable and null otherwise (the CI build step has no deployment database, so they are recorded at deployment); and `known_limitations`, every blocker in `BLOCKERS.md` not marked resolved. `ota_runtime_version` and `image_digests` are present and explicitly null: no over-the-air updates are configured and store builds wait on B07; container images are built and pushed with hosting (B03). *Evidence:* `tests/test_release.py::test_the_manifest_describes_the_build` extended (three distinct app IDs and a version; nulls where nothing exists yet; a 64-character content ID and override snapshot when a database is present; B03 listed as a limitation); the release tests pass locally; full suite in CI. **Not claimed:** a frozen candidate. That needs a deployment (B03), native builds (B07) and the manifest recorded against them. | Engineering (roadmap P19.S4.T3) | B03, B07. |
| PILOT-R0-01 | 2026-10-12 | **The R0 teacher-reviewed pilot switch (W08.S1.T1; roadmap §20.15; provisional, reversible).** *Gap found:* nothing limited written practice to a pilot cohort, so any trial or paid plan's marking units could start written tests, although no written release level beyond R0 is qualified. *Decision:* `PORTAL_WRITTEN_RELEASE` is `r0_pilot` or `open`. Unset, it is `r0_pilot` in staging and production and `open` in development and test (fixtures); an explicit value wins, so leaving the pilot is a recorded configuration decision (WA-D12), not a default. During the pilot, only a learner with an active `pilot` entitlement (granted by a staff member with MFA, audited, and counted separately in the business report) can build, link or start a written test. Anyone else gets 403 `WRITTEN_PILOT_ONLY` with a plain message, and the availability response carries `pilot_only` and `in_pilot`, so web and mobile show a notice instead of the builder. Only pilot entitlements fund pilot tests: a trial or paid plan's units are never reserved, and the `available` figure in an allowance refusal counts only what could have funded the test. Tests already started continue under their pinned terms. Every mark is a teacher's: no automatic publication path exists (AUTOASSESS-01). *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_pilot.py` (role defaults; refused build, start and linked test with no reservation; an existing test keeps uploading; a pilot grant without units does not fall back to the trial; a funded pilot grant is the one reserved). Passed locally with web and mobile type checks and lint; full suite in CI. **Disclosure fix (2026-10-13, found in the browser check):** the plan panel still offered a trial's written units the pilot doesn't let it use, and the trial and price list promised written practice. During the pilot `written_allowance` counts only pilot units; `/v1/me/access` and `/v1/products` carry `written_pilot_only`; web and mobile say written practice is a pilot for invited learners, and each product's units are marked usable once written practice opens beyond the pilot. **Browser-verified locally (desktop):** with the pilot on (a temporary launch entry, removed afterwards): the notice, the plan, trial and price copy, a pilot grant opening the builder, and a start reserved from the pilot entitlement; with it off, nothing changes. **Not done:** cost-backed unit weights (WA-D07 needs measured costs, B06, B10). **Not verified:** on a device. | Engineering (roadmap W08.S1.T1) | WA-D07, WA-D12, B06, B10. |
| INTERRUPTIONS-01 | 2026-10-13 | **Interrupted written journeys keep one receipt (W07.S3.T2).** *Checked, no defect found:* the receipt and status come from the server, so the app closing before the acknowledgement, logging out and signing in again, and a second device of the same account all show the same receipt; a retry with the same key replays it and never makes a second one; another account gets 404. While marks are not released the result says only "pending" (no guessed completion time, no draft marks), and the release notice is sent once however often the release is replayed (one notice per score version). | HISTORY-01 | 2026-10-13 | **Written score history that explains itself (W07.S2.T2).** *Gap found:* the history listed each released version's total and kind, but not what it used or why marks changed. *Fix:* each entry in `versions` adds the decision method, labels (`recheck`, `clearer_copy`, `marking_guide_corrected`, `pending_completed`, `deadline_passed`) and, per question: status, mark, the marking-guide version it was marked under, whether the submitted original or a clearer copy was used, whether it changed from the previous version, and a plain reason for each question the version decided (for example "Marked again by a different teacher after your recheck request.", "Checked again ... the mark stands.", "Marked from the clearer copy you sent after it was pending.", "No clearer copy arrived by the deadline ...", "Marked again under the corrected marking guide."). Carried-forward questions get no reason. Web and mobile show the marking-guide version per version and a line per explained question with the before and after mark. Separate practice attempts (rewrites and new content) were already listed as linked attempts (W04.S3.T3). **Not applicable:** "assisted" work, because written practice has no assisted mode. *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_history.py` (a recheck and an upheld appeal; a clearer copy completing a pending question; a passed deadline), regrade assertions in `tests/test_rubric_adjudication.py`, and `apps/web/e2e/written-results.spec.ts` "a recheck appears in the mark history with its reason" (desktop, run locally). **Not verified:** on a device. | Engineering (roadmap W07.S2.T2) | None. |
| PRACTICE-CONFIG-01 | 2026-10-13 | **Practice configuration: topics and a measured time estimate (P11.S3.T1).** *Gap found:* the API could scope a test to topics but no builder offered them, and nothing told a learner how long a test takes. *Fix:* practice availability lists, per chapter, the topics that have questions with their own counts, and the web and mobile builders let a learner narrow chosen chapters to topics (choosing any limits the whole test to them, as the API does; the count shown follows). Availability also carries `seconds_per_question`: the median time per question on untimed practice tests learners submitted in that class and subject over the last 180 days, ignoring times under 5 seconds or over 30 minutes a question. It stays null until 20 tests exist, and the builders then say "No time estimate yet" instead of guessing. The estimate is shown with how many tests it came from. Already in place: every P09 scope and feedback mode, counts per chapter, and the shortage message with the available count before a test starts. **Client compatibility needs no builder message:** publication refuses a block the oldest supported client can't render without a reviewed fallback (§5.4), and an app below the enforced minimum is told to update before any start (CLIENTS-01). The test in progress keeps its frozen questions and versions; a new test built for the oldest app delivers the block with the same reviewed fallback, which both apps' renderers show in place of an equation they can't typeset (and an unknown block type shows its fallback or an "update the app" notice). *Gap found:* packs leave out any question whose blocks need an archived image (they aren't packed yet), silently. *Fix:* the pack manifest counts them (`questions_online_only`) and the mobile pack card says how many questions with images or diagrams are online only: a clear restriction, never a broken render. | ACADEMIC-REVIEW-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Methods the marking guide doesn't cover go to academic review (W01.S2.T2 escalation, W05.S2.T3; roadmap §20.6.3).** *Gap found:* a teacher who met a valid method the approved guide doesn't list had no route except forcing a score, marking it unassessable, or leaving it pending with nobody accountable. *Decision:* the teacher keeps the question pending with an `escalation` (`unlisted_method` or `rubric_defect`; never together with a learner request). Releasing opens one academic review per question (`written_academic_review`, migration `910fd1af7ed6`, a new table only). While it is open the learner sees that a senior subject reviewer is checking how the guide applies and that nothing counts as zero, and no decision may score the question or close it as unassessable (409 `ACADEMIC_REVIEW_OPEN`). Only an academic adjudicator for the class and subject, in an MFA session, resolves it: `guide_corrected` must name an active approved correction of that question's pinned guide (the correction is then applied as usual before marking), and `not_covered` keeps the guide. Resolving never sets a mark. Raises and resolutions are audited. Reviews are listed in the adjudicator's scope without naming the learner (`/studio/academic-reviews`), and the learner's export includes them without staff identities. **Not covered:** significant-figure, multiple-final-answer and contradiction defaults stay in each approved rubric (§20.6.3 proposes them; no universal rule is invented), and detection of unlisted methods by an automatic assessor needs a provider (B10). *Evidence:* `tests/test_academic_review.py` (the full path with a guide correction; validation; scope; MFA; refusal while open; export) with the privacy, authorization-matrix, upgrade-compatibility, marking, outcome, rescan, history and release tests locally; `alembic check` clean; `apps/web/e2e/written-results.spec.ts` "a method the guide doesn't list waits for academic review, then is marked" and axe on the new page (desktop, local). | Engineering (roadmap W01.S2.T2, W05.S2.T3) | Academic lead (B01) to run it; B10 for automatic detection. |
| UPLOAD-RETRY-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Interruption and concurrency while preparing a script (W03.S3.T3, web).** *Gap found:* an upload lost to the network said "Connection lost. Try again." but offered no way to retry: the learner had to find and choose the file again. *Fix:* a failed upload keeps its file on the device with "Try <file> again" (for a lost connection or a server error) and "Remove from this list". The server recognises the same file (by its hash within the attempt), so a retry after a lost acknowledgement returns the same page, never a second one. Already in place and now exercised in a browser: mapping edits from two tabs get an explicit revision conflict with "Load the latest mapping" (never a silent overwrite), and a reload shows exactly what the server holds. Uploads go straight to the API, so there is no expiring upload URL to recover from. *Evidence:* `apps/web/e2e/written-interruptions.spec.ts` (the upload request dropped, then retried; the same file chosen again; two tabs; a reload; desktop and phone emulation, local), with the existing deduplication and revision tests in `tests/test_written_attempts.py`. **Not verified:** the native app's retry on a device (B09, B14). | Engineering (roadmap W03.S3.T3) | B09, B14 (devices). |
