| QUEUE-AGEING-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Queue ageing per class and subject (W06.S3.T1, part).** *Gap found:* operators saw queued, overdue and capacity figures per scope, but not how long queued reviews had waited (only one global oldest-age signal existed, OPS-01). *Fix:* each `/v1/ops/written-backlog` row counts queued cases waiting under 24 hours, 24 to 48 hours and over 48 hours (the proposed targets in §20.10) and when the longest-waiting case opened; the service page shows it, in red words when any case is over 48 hours. **Not done:** demand forecasting (needs real volumes, B03) and automatic escalation beyond the existing overdue counts, signals and the seven-day learner-remedy deadline (RESCAN-01). *Evidence:* `tests/test_queue_ageing.py` (backdated cases move between the buckets; no-MFA refused) locally; the service page seen in the local browser with local queue data. | Engineering (roadmap W06.S3.T1) | B03 (forecasting). |
| LESSON-PROGRESS-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Lesson progress that never moves backwards (P11.S2.T1).** *Defect found:* the mobile app queues completion changes made offline and replays them later (OFFLINE-SYNC-01), and the server applied them in arrival order, so an "undo" queued on a phone could remove a completion made later on another device. *Fix:* completion and undo take an optional `client_at` (when the learner acted; the mobile queue sends each change's `queued_at`; an immediate action counts as now, and a time in the future counts as now). A per-lesson clock (`lesson_event_clock`) keeps the newest accepted time and ignores older events, so neither a stale undo nor a stale completion reverses a newer one. **Continue reading:** `reading_position` keeps the newest place per chapter (`PUT /v1/me/reading-position`, `GET /v1/me/chapters/{id}/reading-position`), moved only by newer events and never offering a lesson that is no longer published. The web chapter page records the lesson in view and offers "Continue where you left off"; mobile shows "You were last reading" and records the place when a lesson is marked completed. Both tables are new (migration `6b024866201d`) and are in the data export. The dashboard's "continue learning" uses whichever is newer: the saved place or the last opened lesson. *Evidence:* `tests/test_lesson_progress.py` (the dashboard following the newer place; stale undo and completion ignored; a newer undo applies; a fast clock counts as now; positions keep the newest; locked lessons refused; a withdrawn lesson is not offered) with the completion, privacy, isolation, offline-sync, authorization and upgrade-compatibility tests locally; `apps/web/e2e/reading-position.spec.ts` (desktop and phone emulation, another browser context) and the lesson journeys locally. **Not verified:** the mobile queue on a device (B09, B13). | Engineering (roadmap P11.S2.T1) | B09, B13 (devices). |
| IMPORT-REFS-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Imports that reference other items: rubrics, passages and variant families (P06.S2.T1, P08.S3.T2).** *Gap found:* rubrics and passages could only be made in the studio, and imported questions couldn't join a variant family. *Fix:* the structured import accepts `rubric` and `passage` rows. A rubric row names its written question (`question`: external_id or ID) in the same chapter, is attached to it and marks its current version; a question that already has another rubric, an unknown question or one in another chapter is a row error. A question row may name `family` (an existing question in the same chapter, which it joins as a variant; also a CSV column) and `passage` (its `passage_id`). Every row is still only a draft: review, rights and publication gates are unchanged (IMPORT-01). `docs/content-import.md` documents the references. *Evidence:* `tests/test_import_refs.py` (a rubric attached to its question's current version, idempotent re-import, a second rubric and an unknown question refused; a variant joining its family; a passage imported and named by a question; an unknown family refused) with `tests/test_content_import.py` locally. **Not done:** XLSX templates, media manifests and streamed bulk workers. | Engineering (roadmap P06.S2.T1, P08.S3.T2) | None. |
| SECURITY-SUITE-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Consolidated CSRF and XSS checks and an SSRF review (P18.S2.T1, part).** *Checked, no defect found:* `apps/web/e2e/security-boundaries.spec.ts` posts to all nine cookie-authenticated route handlers that change something (sign-out, the written, rescan, screenshot, asset, import, lecture-master, source and resumable uploads), each from a foreign origin and with no origin, and every one answers 403 before it looks at the session (from the app's own origin the same request reaches the session check: 401). The session cookie is HttpOnly and SameSite=Lax. HTML a learner types into a help request is shown as text to the learner and to support staff, with no element created and no script run. Server actions rely on Next.js's own Origin/Host check. `docs/security/ssrf-review.md` lists every server-side outbound request: the web server only calls the configured API origin with escaped or UUID-checked paths, and the API only opens sockets to the configured virus scanner and the database; nothing fetches a user-supplied URL, and no image optimizer remote patterns exist. **Not done:** a record of framework advisories checked (needs an advisory lookup; P18.S4) and HSTS/TLS at the edge (B03). | Engineering (roadmap P18.S2.T1) | B03; P18.S4 review. |
| ROLE-REVOCATION-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Revoked and out-of-scope staff sweeps (P18.S1.T2, part).** *Checked, no defect found:* roles are read from the database on every request, so revoking one takes effect on the next request of the same session. `tests/test_role_revocation.py` grants support, finance, platform operator and a scoped subject reviewer through the owner's staff screen API, shows each using an endpoint it needs, revokes it, and the same session is refused at once (403). A reviewer scoped to another subject or another class neither sees a Class XII Chemistry script in the queue nor opens or leases its case. | Engineering (roadmap P18.S1.T2) | Independent review (P18.S4). |

Trimming stays on the web. **Status:** implemented, mobile typecheck and lint only. **Not verified:** on a device (B09, B14); Expo web is not device verification. | Engineering (roadmap W03.S1.T3) | B09, B14 (devices). |
| ADVISORY-REVIEW-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Dependency and framework advisory review (P18.S2.T3, P18.S4.T3 part).** `docs/security/advisory-review.md`: pip-audit on the 38 API runtime packages (none), `pnpm audit --prod` at every severity (the two reviewed high exceptions plus two new moderate ones, both mobile-only), and a GitHub advisory database look-up of each main web, mobile and API framework version (none; the query was first confirmed on known-affected versions). *Residual risks, with owner and proposed target date 2026-12-31:* decode-uri-component (GHSA-vcc3-ghjq-m6fr) via expo-router, a device-local hang on a malformed link, not patched because the fixed release is ESM-only and cannot be device-verified (B09, B14); uuid (GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq) in prebuild tooling only. | IMPORT-XLSX-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Excel workbooks and downloadable templates for the multiple-choice import (P06.S2.T1, part).** *Gap found:* authors had to re-save Excel sheets as CSV, and there was no template to start from. *Fix:* `format=xlsx` reads the first worksheet's saved values into the same row validation as CSV (same drafts, same idempotency). The reader uses only the standard library. Before any row is read it caps each part at 25 MB (zip bombs), refuses any DOCTYPE or ENTITY (entity expansion), and allows at most 64 columns and 2,000 rows; formulas are never evaluated, and a column with values but no heading is an error. Repeated headings are now refused for CSV too. `GET /v1/studio/imports/templates/{mcq.xlsx|mcq.csv}` serves headings-only templates to content authors (workbook columns formatted as text, heading row frozen); the studio import page links both and accepts `.xlsx`. Migration `219df9c8c75c` widens `content_import_batch_format` to allow `xlsx`. *Rollback:* an earlier build lists only json/csv batches; an xlsx batch holds exactly the CSV template's rows, so run `UPDATE content_import_batch SET format = 'csv' WHERE format = 'xlsx'` before running it (the downgrade does the same). **Tests:** `test_import_xlsx.py` (3); E2E `studio.spec.ts` (template download, workbook upload); the template also opens in openpyxl 3.1.5, and a workbook re-saved by openpyxl reads back with `007` intact. **Not verified:** opening in Microsoft Excel itself. | Engineering (roadmap P06.S2.T1) | — |
| MEDIA-MANIFEST-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Media packs for the asset archive (P06.S2.T1 media manifests, P07.S2.T4).** *Gap found:* diagrams, handouts and captions could only be archived one file at a time. *Fix:* `POST /v1/studio/assets/manifest?commit=` takes a .zip (at most 60 MB) with `manifest.json` (schema 1: class, subject, at most 200 items, each with `external_id`, kind, title, file, optional sha256 and provenance). Every file is read with its kind's size cap, checked against its checksum, and scanned and validated exactly like a single upload; file names are exact paths inside the pack. Entries are keyed by `external_id` within the class and subject (`content_asset.external_ref`, partial unique index; migration `3c0d0a813dc8`), so a re-import is unchanged and a changed file becomes the next immutable version; a key can't change kind. A preview writes nothing. A commit re-checks everything and archives every file in one transaction, or none; it is refused (`MANIFEST_ERRORS`) while any entry has an error, and a storage failure part-way takes back every written file (`assets.new_version`/`remove_files`, shared with single uploads). Only content authors in the pack's scope can use it. The studio assets page has a Media pack section (check, then archive) showing each file's version ID, and the archive list shows pack keys. **Tests:** `test_media_manifest.py` (4, including the part-way storage failure); `test_assets.py` still passes; E2E `studio.spec.ts` (check, archive, re-import unchanged); the new route handler is in the CSRF list (`security-boundaries.spec.ts`). Real object storage waits on B03. | Engineering (roadmap P06.S2.T1) | Object storage (B03). |
| IMPORT-BULK-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Large imports checked and committed by an import worker (P06.S2.T1 "stream large jobs in bulk workers").** *Gap found:* a preview ran inside the request, so files over 5 MB or 2,000 rows had to be split by hand, and a commit of thousands of rows would have outlived the request. *Fix:* `POST /v1/studio/imports/bulk` streams the file to private storage (`PORTAL_IMPORT_DIR`; at most 50 MB and 20,000 rows; the same hourly preview budget) and queues the batch (202). `portal-import-worker` claims it with `SKIP LOCKED` and a two-minute lease and checks rows with the preview's own rules (`imports._validate_row`). It stores each chunk of 250 rows in its own transaction and extends the lease, so a crashed worker's batch resumes after its last stored row with nothing checked twice. It then marks duplicates across the whole file (`imports.mark_duplicates`, shared with previews), and the batch becomes an ordinary preview that expires a day later. Committing more than 2,000 rows is queued too: the worker re-checks and writes every row in one transaction, or none (`imports.apply_commit`, shared with the request path). A row changed since the preview, or an item created meanwhile, fails the batch with the reason and nothing written. The upload is deleted once its rows are stored, or on failure or cancel. Polls get progress without rows. The studio import page switches to this path by itself (over 5 MB, or after a 413 for too many rows), shows Queued and "Checking rows: N of M", lets the author cancel a queued batch, and lists only rows with errors or warnings past 500. A **Your recent imports** list on the import page reopens any batch (`?batch=`), so an author can leave while the worker runs and come back (added in the follow-up PR with CAPTURE-01). Migration `8d67f124450b`: six nullable columns and four statuses (queued, validating, committing, failed). *Rollback:* set those statuses to `discarded` first (none has written content; the downgrade does the same). Operations: signal `content_import_queue_age_s` (warn 15 min, alert 1 h; runbook `import-backlog`); the worker is in the bulk capacity class; CI starts it for the browser journeys. **Tests:** `test_bulk_import.py` (4: worker preview and commit, resume after a crash part-way, all-or-none commit, broken, cancelled and oversized files, access); E2E `studio.spec.ts` (2,001 rows through the worker, progress, the 500-row cap, discard). **Deployment:** `portal-import-worker` must run as its own process, with `PORTAL_IMPORT_DIR` on private durable storage (B03). | Engineering (roadmap P06.S2.T1) | Object storage (B03). |
| CAPTURE-01 | 2026-10-14 | **Native capture: pages kept on the phone until they upload, PDF import, low-storage and camera-permission states (W03.S1.T2).** *Gap found:* the mobile written screen sent each photo straight from the picker's cache, so a failed upload, lost signal or a closed app lost the page (a camera photo is nowhere else). PDFs couldn't be chosen, a full phone wasn't detected, and a denied camera gave only a line of text. *Fix:* `src/lib/page-drafts.ts` copies every page into the app's private document storage first, filed under the account (ISOLATION-01) and the test; the record is written last, so an interrupted save is tidied away. A kept page is removed once the server has it. The screen lists kept pages as **On this phone, not sent yet** (Send again, Remove), apart from Uploaded (not submitted) and Submitted. Kept pages are sent again when the test is opened, deleted when it is no longer open, and deleted at sign-out. Free space is checked first (`Paths.availableDiskSpace`, keeping 50 MB free); on a nearly full phone, or a copy failing for space, the screen says so and still sends the page straight away, warning that it isn't kept. **Choose a PDF** uses the system document picker (`File.pickFileAsync`; no new dependency). Size limits (15 MB per image, 40 MB per PDF) are checked before anything is copied. A denied camera shows a notice with **Open settings** and the other ways to add pages. The Expo web preview sends pages straight away and keeps nothing (`page-drafts.web.ts`). **Status:** implemented; mobile typecheck, lint, `expo config`, and the JavaScript bundles for Android and web build (`expo export`; a compile check, not device evidence). **Not verified:** on a device (B09, B14), including real low-storage and permission prompts; Expo web is not device verification. | Engineering (roadmap W03.S1.T2) | B09, B14 (devices). |
| EXPO-CHECK-01 | 2026-10-14 | **The mobile CI "Expo dependency/config checks" step checked nothing.** *Found:* it ran `pnpm doctor`, which is pnpm's own built-in command (it shadows the package's `doctor` script), and `expo-doctor` was never installed, so the step always passed without looking at Expo. *Fix:* the step now runs `npx expo install --check` as a labelled, non-blocking drift report: it compares with Expo's newest patch releases online, so it would otherwise fail whenever Expo publishes one. A new blocking step checks that the app config resolves (`npx expo config --type public`). The package's `doctor` script runs the same check (`pnpm run doctor`). On 2026-10-14 it reported five patch updates (expo 57.0.28, expo-router 57.0.26, @expo/ui 57.0.23, expo-crypto 57.0.4, expo-splash-screen 57.0.10). They are not applied here: the app's packages are updated together with a device build (B09, B14). | Engineering (CI honesty) | B09, B14 for the updates. |
| OCT14-01 | 2026-10-15 | **A written test could be submitted with unsent pages (review of 2026-10-14).** *Reproduced* from the source: Submit checked only the answer map and `saving`, and a closed test then cleared the phone's kept pages. *Fix:* the page and submit rules moved to `apps/mobile/src/lib/written-pages.ts`. Submitting is refused, inside `seal()` itself, while any page is unsent or anything is in flight: choosing, copying, sending, retrying, saving the map or turning a page. Captures are refused once submitting starts. The submission key is kept, so a retry after a lost response returns the same receipt. A closed test's leftover pages are listed with an explicit Remove, never deleted silently. **Tests:** `apps/mobile/tests/written-pages.test.ts` (host-side, injected timing; mutation-checked). Not device evidence (B09, B14). | Engineering (review OCT14) | Devices (B09, B14). |
| OCT14-02 | 2026-10-15 | **A stale import worker could overwrite a takeover.** *Reproduced on PostgreSQL:* worker A's lease ran out, worker B took over and finished the preview, then A marked it failed. A second gap: sessions keep objects across commits (`expire_on_commit=False`), so A's lock re-read trusted what its session remembered. *Fix:* each claim carries a token in `lease_owner`. Every write (progress, preview, commit, failure, file cleanup and audit) re-reads the batch under a lock with `populate_existing` and stops (`Stop`) unless the token and status still match. Files are deleted only after the state change that releases them commits. **Tests:** `test_import_worker_hardening.py` (two sessions: takeover before A starts, between chunks, and during commit; mutation-checked). | Engineering (review OCT14) | — |
| OCT14-03 | 2026-10-15 | **A damaged workbook stopped the import worker.** *Reproduced:* a damaged deflate stream raised `zlib.error`, and a CRC mismatch raised `BadZipFile`. Neither was an application error, and the drain stopped with the batch left validating. *Fix:* damaged, encrypted or unsupported members of a workbook or a media-pack manifest are now validation errors (`ZIP_MEMBER_ERRORS`). In the worker, bad input fails once with its reason. Any other error releases the batch for a retry after 30 s, doubling up to 15 min, and fails it after `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5) claims, counted even when a worker dies (migration `1311c92533d7`: `attempts`, `last_error`). One batch's trouble never stops the queue. **Tests:** CRC, encrypted and unknown-compression workbooks fail and a following batch succeeds; a damaged manifest gives `MEDIA_PACK`; retries pause and then fail; a batch that keeps killing its worker is failed. | Engineering (review OCT14) | — |
| OCT14-04 | 2026-10-15 | **Recovery could delete an unsent page.** *Reproduced* from the source, as in the review's fault-injected probe: an error note was written over the page's only record, and recovery deleted an unreadable record and then the page. *Fix:* `apps/mobile/src/lib/page-drafts-core.ts` (file system injected): pages are copied in as `.part` and renamed once complete; the record is written once (tmp then rename); notes go in a separate file replaced the same way. Recovery never deletes a page: one whose record is missing or unreadable is shown as a recovered page, its type read from the file. **Tests:** `apps/mobile/tests/page-drafts.test.ts` (truncated writes, failed copies, no space, repeated reopen, old-format records; mutation-checked). Not device evidence. | Engineering (review OCT14) | Devices (B09, B14). |
| OCT14-05 | 2026-10-15 | **Queued imports ran for a suspended author.** *Reproduced:* the worker's actor helper returned an actor for a suspended account. *Fix:* the worker requires an active account, re-read for every chunk and at commit, and re-reads scoped roles for every chunk. A refusal says why ("no longer active", or "can no longer be imported: ..."), and nothing new is written. Reinstating an author doesn't revive a failed batch; they upload again. **Tests:** suspension before the commit and before validation, a revoked role, reinstatement, and an active author's success. | Engineering (review OCT14) | — |
| OCT14-06 | 2026-10-15 | **The DOCTYPE and entity guard depended on encoding.** *Reproduced:* a UTF-16 workbook part declaring an entity was parsed and expanded. *Fix:* the byte regex is gone. `imports.parse_xml` builds the tree with expat, whose DOCTYPE, entity, notation and external-entity handlers all refuse after decoding, so the rule holds in any encoding. **Tests:** internal entity, external DTD and bare DOCTYPE, each refused in UTF-8, UTF-16 LE and UTF-16 BE; a valid workbook whose parts are UTF-16 still reads. | Engineering (review OCT14) | — |

| STRAIGHTEN-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Straightening crooked page photos (W03.S1.T3 deskew), behind a rollout until qualified.** *Gap:* only quarter turns existed. *Built:* `evidence.estimate_skew` finds the angle from the writing itself, using a projection profile tried every half degree within ±10° and then refined to a tenth, in the isolated page worker. Straightening samples nearest-neighbour with expand, so every pixel shown is an original pixel or paper white, nothing is blended lighter and nothing is cut off. The transform stores `straighten` with `aspect`, the page's shape before straightening, which region mapping needs. A trim made on a straightened page is kept in that page's own terms (`shown_crop`) and reset when the angle changes. `to_source` maps markers' regions, detail views and trims back through straightening, turning and cropping. The web and mobile page controls gain Straighten and Undo straighten, shown only where the `page_straighten` rollout is on: development 100%, staging and production 0%, and staff always. Keeping or undoing an angle is always allowed. **Why it is gated:** resampling can drop pixels of a 1 px stroke at some angles. W03.S1.T3 requires qualification on real faint-ink and pencil scripts (B10) before learners get it; markers can always see the untouched original. **Tests:** `test_page_straighten.py` (the angle found for ±tilts; level and blank pages untouched; only original pixels shown and faint strokes kept within ±10%; regions mapped back through crop, turn, straighten and `shown_crop` against a marked dot; checks; the API flow with the rollout off and on, keep, trim, undo and the marker's detail views); E2E `practice.spec.ts` (straighten, keep through a turn, undo; synthetic tilted fixture). | Engineering (roadmap W03.S1.T3) | Qualification on real scripts (B10). |
| DEMAND-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Measured review demand (W06.S3.T1).** Found by the register reconciliation for the review of 2026-10-14: W06.S3.T1's forecast named no gate. *Built:* `review.review_demand` and a `demand` block on each backlog row, applying roadmap §20.13.3 to data the system already records. The recheck rate q is rechecks opened over first markings released in the last 90 days, shown only once at least 20 were released. Rechecks still expected (R) are q times the first markings released in the 14-day recheck window. Queued work is broken down by kind. With staffing recorded, the forecast gives the minutes those rechecks would take and the admissions that would fit if they were subtracted. **A forecast only:** admission (STAFFING-01) is unchanged, because counting R before admitting is part of WA-D08 (owner). | WRITTEN-CONNECTION-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Written tests through a lost connection or a changed clock (W04.S2.T1).** Several fixes in the written test runner. The countdown's offset to server time is measured again whenever a fresh server time arrives; before, it was measured once. Back online, or back on the tab, the runner fetches the attempt again: a fresh server time and its real status. A written-specific notice says what an outage means (time keeps running, the upload deadline, pages can't upload, a failed page stays in the tab, page choices are sent when the connection is back). The global banner speaks only of MCQ answers. A page map that couldn't be saved is sent again on reconnect. Submitting saves the map first instead of refusing for an earlier failure; a failed pre-submit save now shows as an error, not "Saving…". *Defect found and fixed:* as in ANSWER-STATES-01, the map save, submit and page-adjust server actions rejected when the request never reached the server, so "Saving…" or "Submitting…" could hang. They now go through the shared `lib/reach.ts`. **Tests:** Playwright `written-interruptions.spec.ts`, run on desktop and phone. With `page.clock`, the device clock jumps 20 minutes and is corrected when the learner returns to the tab. Offline: the notice shows, and a page choice and a submit are both refused with a reason. Back online: the map is saved and the script submitted. Each stage has a median, a 90th percentile and its count. **Outcomes, counted apart** so a latency is never read without its denominator: started, accepted, not submitted (and the plan-withdrawn share), still writing, pending, delayed (an open case past due), released by a teacher, released automatically (always 0 in R0). Both apps use the same `lib/answer-state.ts`, kept identical by a host test. *Defect found while testing (web):* a server-action request that never reached the server rejected instead of returning a failure. So an offline save had no retry, the page showed "Saving…" indefinitely, and an offline submit could stay on "Submitting…". In development, the reconnect listener was also lost when React ran the restore effect twice. *Fix:* every runner request goes through `reach()`, which turns a failed request into a failure result. The reconnect listener has its own effect. A failed submit restarts the retry timer (web and mobile). Closing the dialog clears an old submit error. **Tests:** `apps/mobile/tests/answer-state.test.ts` (5); Playwright `practice.spec.ts` outage journey: pending while offline, an offline submit refused with "Try again", saved after reconnect; desktop and phone. | NEAR-DUP-01 | 2026-10-15 | **The same page uploaded twice is flagged, never removed (W03.S2.T3).** An identical file was already recognised by its hash. A page photographed or exported again (re-compressed, resized, lighter or darker) has different bytes. So the evidence worker now stores each page's 32 × 32 grey thumbnail as a fingerprint (`written_page.fingerprint`, migration `5c86c03c84e3`). At upload, it is compared with the pages already in the script after normalising brightness and contrast (mean 0, spread 1). A mean difference of at most 0.45 gives a `near_duplicate` quality flag, level `possible`, with `like_page_id`. The learner is told to choose only one copy for their answers, and which page number it resembles; the marker sees the flag among the photo checks. Nothing is removed or merged. Blank sheets get no fingerprint and are never flagged. The flag survives turning or trimming the page. *Calibration (synthetic only):* a first attempt with min–max contrast scaling missed a re-saved copy of the e2e fixture page (difference 12.7 against a threshold of 8), so it was replaced by mean–spread scaling. Measured over the e2e fixture pages and seeded synthetic writing: the same page re-encoded, resized, lightened, darkened or with more contrast differs by at most 0.26; different pages by at least 0.74. **Provisional** until real scans (B10). **Tests:** `test_near_duplicate_pages.py` (3); Playwright `practice.spec.ts` (a re-saved JPEG of the fixture page is flagged as like page 1, axe); the written upload, quality, transform, straighten, rescan and feedback tests pass. | Engineering (roadmap W03.S2.T3) | Threshold calibration on real scans (B10) |
| ATTEMPT-STATES-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Attempt states (P10.S1.T1): how the roadmap's states map onto the model,** recorded for the register reconciliation. Prepared: a built form before its attempt starts. Active. Finalising: inside the single finalise transaction, never observable. Submitted or expired: `finalised` with a reason. Scoring and scored: the score version (with recoverable scoring pending, RECOVERABLE-SCORING-01). NOT_SCORABLE: a zero maximum. Superseded result: a newer score version. Void: the `void` status exists in the schema, but nothing sets it, because the roadmap defines no rule for voiding a whole attempt; void questions are handled by quarantine (SOFT, VOID, KEY_ERROR). A staff action to void an attempt (for example after an integrity incident) needs that rule first, an owner decision. | Records (review OCT14) | Owner: when an attempt may be voided. |
| POOL-CAPS-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Worker classes are enforced in code, and bulk work waits out mock windows (P17.S2.T2).** The §5.8 budget gave each command a class and a per-process pool, but every process used the API's default pool of 10, and a deployment had to set `PORTAL_DB_POOL_SIZE` per worker by hand. Now each installed command is wrapped in `budget.process_class(<class>)`. Before it runs, this caps the process's pool at its class's "pool each" (critical 5, standard 3, bulk 3, reserve 7, written classes 3 or 2); `PORTAL_DB_POOL_SIZE` can only lower it. A process that already has a pool keeps it, because the cap is for the fresh deployed process. `test_pool_caps.py` fails if any command in `ENTRY_POINTS` lacks the declaration or names another class. **Mock windows:** from 15 minutes before a scheduled mock window opens until 15 minutes after it closes, the import and export workers claim no new work, and the scheduled bulk jobs skip that run: asset retention, upload reconciliation and import reconciliation with `--apply`. Queued work simply waits. Operator-run imports are not paused. `PORTAL_BULK_PAUSE_IN_MOCK_WINDOWS=false` turns the pause off. **Tests:** `test_pool_caps.py` (every command declared; a fresh process gets 3 for bulk and a running one keeps its pool; the pause before the window, the off switch, and no pause after the margin), `test_budget.py`, and the tests of every module that gained a declaration (50 passed). | Engineering (roadmap P17.S2.T2) | Separate deployments and queues per class, and CPU/memory caps, need hosting (B03) |
| CLIENT-FLOOR-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Publication checks use the oldest supported build's manifest (P13.S1.T4, §5.4 rule 4; the "not done" part of CLIENTS-01).** `clients.renderer_floor(db)` gives, per native platform, the renderer version of the oldest supported build: the lowest renderer among registered manifests at or above the minimum version in effect now. A raised minimum still in its notice period isn't in effect yet, so the builds it will drop still count. To stay never less careful than before, the code's lower bound (`SUPPORTED_RENDERER`) is kept in unless the minimum version itself has a manifest. Builds are assumed to raise their renderer version, never lower it. The web app is always current. The floor applies through a context-scoped value (`blocks.client_floor`) to publication and draft validation (including linked rubric and passage checks), the form and surface previews ("what the oldest supported client shows") and the studio block registry. Until manifests are registered from a release pipeline (B03), the result equals the code constant, so nothing changes today. **Tests:** `test_client_floor.py`. With fixture manifests 89.0.0 (renderer 1), 90.0.0 (2) and 90.1.0 (3), a minimum of 90.0.0 in effect gives android 2, and the block registry reports it. A minimum of 90.0.5 with no manifest gives the lower bound. Restoring the minimum gives the lower bound again. A floor older than every block makes checks require fallbacks. The client policy, content workflow, form preview, surface preview and lesson block tests pass. | Engineering (roadmap P13.S1.T4) | Manifests registered by a release pipeline (B03); D08 minimum OS (B09) |
| MODULES-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Module boundaries written down and checked (P01.S1.T2).** `docs/architecture/modules.md` places the 13 API modules in layers. **Core:** config, db, errors, days, observability, budget. **Foundation:** identity, audit, curriculum, notifications, access, exports, analytics, system. **Features:** content, assessment, written, support, help. **Entry points:** main, export_openapi, release. The page says what each module owns and how modules call each other (plain functions, never HTTP; a module's tables are written only through its own functions). `test_module_boundaries.py` reads the module-level import graph; imports inside functions are the documented way to cross a layer without an import-time dependency. It enforces: core imports no module; only entry points import the app; routers are leaves; foundation modules don't import feature modules; no new import-time cycles (two exist today and are listed: audit ↔ identity, assessment ↔ written); and every module has a layer. | COMPARISON-01 | 2026-10-15 | **Learner comparisons are honest, and only for fixed forms (P12.S2.T3, §6.6, AC62; #219).** Only a scheduled session's fixed form (FIXED-FORMS-01) is compared; practice tests and practice mocks are varied per learner and never compared. **Membership:** one attempt per eligible learner account, the earliest qualifying one; eligible = active, no staff role, not a development/test account, finalised within the 90 days before the snapshot, latest score `scored` (void and NOT_SCORABLE left out). Learners with an accommodation are compared only with each other. **Comparable scores:** every member's latest score must use the current scoring policy and the form's current effective correction set; otherwise (a correction still being applied, or a submission still being scored) the snapshot is `pending` and nothing is shown, so scores from before and after a correction are never mixed. **Threshold:** fewer than 200 distinct accounts is `too_few`, and the learner is told about the sample limitation instead. **Percentile:** 100 x (lower + 0.5 x equal) / N with the learner included, rounded half-up to one decimal, shown with N, the window, the tie rule and the snapshot version, and the note that accounts are not proof of distinct people and the cohort is not a national sample. **Snapshots** (`comparison_snapshot`, `comparison_member`, migration `b780d235c059`) are built atomically by `portal-comparison-snapshots` (class `bulk`) for sessions whose results are released and still inside the window; unchanged inputs make no new version; the result endpoint reads only the latest snapshot and tells a learner whose score changed after it that the comparison is being updated. Web and mobile result screens show it. | WORKLOAD-GUARD-01 | 2026-10-15 | **A workload guard pauses written work while MCQ exams are under strain (W09.S1.T3, §20.11.1, WA-AC68; #220).** Every served API process publishes its MCQ save health (saves, saves over the 1 s budget, 5xx saves, pool use) every 20 s (`PORTAL_HEALTH_SAMPLE_S`; 0 = off). One `workload_guard` row, changed under its row lock with at most one evaluation per 20 s across processes, reads the last minute of samples, the database's connection use (`pg_stat_activity`) and MCQ submissions waiting over 2 minutes for a score. Rules need evidence: slow saves count only with at least 30 saves in the minute and more than 5% over budget; failing saves need at least 3 and 1%. **Hysteresis:** 90% database connections pause at once; other faults after 3 consecutive checks; recovery needs 3 healthy checks after the last fault and 5 minutes without one, then ramps 25%, 50%, healthy; any fault while recovering pauses again. Every change is audited with its reasons. **While paused:** building or starting a new written test returns 503 `WRITTEN_PAUSED` (Retry-After 120); reopening an admitted test, uploads, sealing and receipts are never refused (reserved capacity for admitted work); the regrade and automatic-assessment workers take no new work (their ramp share while recovering); bulk work waits (`bulk_pause_reason`). Learners see a notice on the written page (web and app); operators see `written_guard_state` and the `workload-guard` runbook section. The CI e2e job turns the sampler off (the latency rules are for real traffic; a shared runner would trip them at random); the database and scoring rules stay on. Thresholds are proposed operating controls, not measured ones. Tests: `test_workload_guard.py` (6; 11 single mutations each fail one). | EXAMPROFILE-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Versioned exam profiles with two-person verification (P05.S3.T1-T3).** An exam profile is an official pattern; MDCAT and each UET ECAT combination are separate profiles. Practice tests and practice availability never include mock-reserved questions. | SCHEDULE-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Scheduled mock windows (P09.S2.T3).** Staff with `manage_exam_profiles` (MFA) schedule a session for the current published version of a pattern. A session has a start, a late-entry cutoff, a window close and a results-release time; times are stored in UTC and shown in the session's timezone (default Asia/Karachi). Validation: future start; order start < entry close ≤ window close; a fixed-end window at least as long as the test; results no earlier than the window close. **Late entry:** `fixed_end` gives everyone the same end, so late entrants get less time; `full_duration` gives the full time from joining, capped at the window close. **Accommodations:** extra minutes for one learner in one session, with a reason, audited. **Joining:** once per session (idempotent). Joining builds the frozen mock form, with the time limit computed and frozen at join, and starts the attempt. Late entry after the cutoff is refused (`ENTRY_CLOSED`), but anyone already in can return. **Results:** withheld entirely until the session's release time (409 `RESULTS_PENDING` with the time), because per-question marks would reveal keys to others still in the window. The release time is read live from the session, so staff can delay it, for example for a correction; the receipt still confirms submission. | NOTEBOOK-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Mistake notebook with spaced review (P12.S3.T1-T3).** **Collecting:** every question answered wrongly in a submitted practice test, mock or review test becomes or refreshes one entry per question family. Blanks are not counted as mistakes. Entries are recorded in the submission transaction and again on any re-score. **Voids:** when a reviewed correction voids a question (EXCLUDE or CREDIT_ALL), its entry stays as `voided` with the reason. **Spacing:** deterministic intervals of 1, 3, 7 and 14 days. A miss, first time or in review, resets the entry to 1 day; a correct review answer moves it to the next interval; after the last interval it is `mastered`. Every entry explains why it is due; overdue entries are simply due, and missed days never pile up extra work. **Review tests:** `kind=review`, at most 20 questions, one class and subject. They prefer a variant other than the version missed, and the original attempt is never changed. Learners keep private notes on entries. *Provisional:* the intervals are fixed defaults (the roadmap allows performance adjustment). Flagged or low-confidence answers aren't collected, because the attempt protocol has no flag yet. The mobile notebook is pending. | Engineering (roadmap P12.S3) | — |

Withdrawn and credited-to-everyone questions don't count as answered. Also included: the 30 most recent results with score version and an "updated after review" note, and mistake-notebook counts. | EVIDENCE-RULES-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Learning evidence under `evidence_rules_v2` (§6.6, P12.S2.T1/T2/T4).** It is implemented as a pure, versioned function (`assessment/evidence.py`) so every rule is tested exactly. **Evidence unit:** each eligible finalised response under its latest score version. **Window:** 90 x 24 hours. **Weights:** age (1 up to 45 days, 0.5 up to 90) x hint (0.5) x exposure (0.25 if the same canonical family was answered in the previous 30 days). **Cap:** one family contributes at most 2 per outcome. **Independent subset:** unhinted and unexposed. **Classification, first match:** `insufficient_evidence` if weight < 6 or fewer than 3 families; `demonstrated` if independent weight ≥ 6 from ≥ 3 families, independent accuracy ≥ 80% and ≥ 2 recent correct families; otherwise `developing`, always with reasons. Old strong evidence needs recent confirmation. Total accuracy only guides revision. **Exclusions:** staff accounts, blanks, and withdrawn or credited questions give no evidence. Cold start shows "not enough evidence", never an invented ability. **Meters:** demonstrated knowledge is shown; syllabus coverage and content completed show **unavailable**, because their denominators aren't honestly computable yet. Every report carries the rule version and evaluation time. *Provisional:* an outcome is the question's topic (else chapter) until verified exam outcomes exist (B02); the hint factor is always 1 because there are no hints; peer percentiles (P12.S2.T3) wait for ≥ 200 eligible learners. | Engineering (roadmap §6.6, P12.S2) | B02 outcome mapping. |
| STUDYPLAN-01 | 2026-10-09 | **Time-budgeted study plan (P12.S4.T1, §6.6 infeasible deadlines).** `GET /v1/me/study-plan?grade=&subject=&target_date=[&daily_minutes=]` and `/practice/plan`. Daily minutes come from the request or the learner's profile, and the plan asks for them if neither has a value. **Required minutes:** each topic of the book needs work by its evidence state (demonstrated 0, developing 30, no evidence 45). **Available minutes:** days to the target times daily minutes. **Priority:** developing topics with the lowest first-time accuracy first, then other developing topics, then topics without evidence, with ties in the book's chapter and topic order (the prerequisite order until a verified map exists). Topics are scheduled whole into days. If required exceeds available, the shortfall is stated and only the highest-priority work that fits is scheduled; nothing implies full completion. The plan is recomputed from current evidence every time, so missed days replan without a backlog. It never changes official-pattern mocks and never predicts admission. *Provisional:* the minute estimates are transparent defaults; exam-weight priority needs verified profile outcome weights (B02). | Engineering (roadmap P12.S4.T1) | B02 weights. |

New permission `manage_catalogue` (owner/admin; MFA). `POST /v1/admin/catalogue/preview` returns every chapter/topic that would be added, retired, reactivated, renamed, moved or reordered (by stable ID with old and new values, from `importer.plan`/`describe`), plus the content linked to retiring or moving structure, flagging published items. `POST /v1/admin/catalogue/apply` runs the import only if the file's SHA-256 matches the preview (`CATALOGUE_CHANGED`) and, when published content is affected, only with explicit acknowledgement (`DEPENDENCIES_UNACKNOWLEDGED`). Nothing is deleted; reapplying an earlier catalogue reactivates retired structure. Both steps are audited. A variant is created with `translation_of` and is its own item, chapter and topic taken from the original, with its own draft → review → publish workflow, so each language has a separate review status; one live-or-pending version per language per concept (`LANGUAGE_TAKEN`); the origin must be stated. No machine translation service exists or is called; `machine_draft` only labels text an author brings in, and it still needs the independent review. Learners: `GET /v1/chapters/{id}/lessons?language=` lists each concept once, shows the reviewed variant in that language, or another (English first) with `requested_language_missing` and `available_languages`, so a missing translation is explicit and never filled in. Studio `GET /v1/studio/items/{id}/variants` and a Language versions panel; web chapter page has a language choice, a missing-language note and right-to-left Urdu. **Mobile (follow-up):** the native chapter screen has the same language choice and missing-language note (verified on Expo web only, not a device). P07.S1.T3 checkpoints followed in CHECKPOINTS-01. | Reversible |
| CHECKPOINTS-01 | 2026-10-10 | **Formative lesson checkpoints (P07.S1.T3).** New registered block `checkpoint` (schema v1): `question` (2 to 6 options, one key, an explanation shown after answering) or `self_check` (a prompt and what a good answer contains, revealed on request). Rendered on web and native (radio semantics, live region); the studio block editor adds them (options one per line, `*` marks the key). **Policy:** checkpoints are self-checks: nothing is sent, stored or scored, they never feed `evidence_rules_v2`, and bank questions are never reused in them (the key travels with the lesson, so bank-key isolation is unaffected). Lesson completion stays learner-marked (LESSON-DONE-01) and is the only input to the content-completed meter. **Renderer note:** the block is renderer v1 on every platform because no native build has shipped (B07); after a store release, new block types must raise the renderer version. | Reversible |

Aggregates only, every figure defined: learners (active accounts without a staff role), active in 7/30 days, access now by entitlement source, 12 weeks of trial cohorts by ISO start week (started, running, ended, converted to paid; a cohort with running trials is marked open, never counted as non-conversion), and 30-day content usage (lessons completed, tests submitted, written tests sealed, engaged learners). **Test accounts:** accounts from the development identity adapter are counted separately and excluded from every other figure. **Payments:** verified purchases, refunds and revenue are reported `available: false` with blocker B06 until a payment provider confirms payments; revenue is never estimated. | Reversible |
| SERVICE-OVERVIEW-01 | 2026-10-10 | **Service overview (P16.S1.T3).** The correlation middleware also records each request (route template, status, duration; never IDs or personal data) into a per-process rolling five-minute window. Three signals join OPS-01: `http_server_error_ratio_5m` (warn 1%, alert 5%), `http_latency_p95_ms_5m` (warn 800 ms, alert 2 s) and `submission_server_error_ratio_5m` for MCQ submit and written seal (warn 0.1%, alert 1%; a 409 refusal is a decision, not a failure). Signals nothing can measure yet are listed as `unavailable` with their blocker: video playback failures (B05) and device-journey budget breaches (B09). Every signal links to a runbook section, and a test checks each anchor exists. Web `/admin/service` for platform operators. **Provisional:** thresholds are engineering defaults, not validated SLOs (load evidence waits on B03); the window is per process, so a multi-process deployment needs its monitoring to aggregate. | Reversible |
| HISTORY-CHECK-01 | 2026-10-10 | **Large-history report check (review OCT9 follow-up).** `tests/test_large_history.py` clones one real submitted test into 1,000 more finalised attempts for one fixture learner (set-based SQL) and times the reports: locally, progress 0.15 s and evidence 0.38 s at 1,001 attempts (development laptop, Postgres in Docker). The test fails above 3 s, which would catch per-attempt queries or quadratic work; with the query-count test, report cost is bounded by one pass over history. This is engineering evidence only: no production latency or scale claim (B03). | Engineering |
| ANALYTICS-EVENTS-01 | 2026-10-10 | **Analytics event dictionary (P16.S2.T1).** `analytics/events.py` `DICTIONARY` defines each event's version, meaning, source and exact typed properties; `emit` refuses unknown events, unknown or missing properties, wrong types and values outside a property's choices. Event IDs are uuid5 of name and source object, inserted `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so retries and replayed operations record once. Server events are written in the same transaction as the work: `attempt.started` (only the request that created the attempt), `answer.saved` (admission state, cleared or not, never the option), `attempt.submitted`, `score.version_created` (first scoring and corrections), `lesson.completed`, `trial.decided` (per request key and outcome). Client: `lesson.started` via `POST /v1/me/events/lesson-started` (live lessons only; once per lesson per day), sent by a web beacon when a signed-in learner sees a readable lesson. Defined but not emitted, with reasons: `purchase.completed` (B06) and `checkpoint.answered` (checkpoints are never stored, CHECKPOINTS-01). Property names may not contain private words (note, password, token, email, name, phone, option, answer, key, stem, text, body). Events carry the account ID only and are in the personal-data export. Dictionary readable at `GET /v1/admin/analytics/dictionary` (owner/admin, finance; MFA). Funnel reports (P16.S2.T2) build on this. | Reversible |

| HANDOFF-01 | 2026-10-11 | **One device answers at a time (P10.S1.T3).** An active attempt records the signed-in device that holds it (`attempt.holder_session_id`, migration `c8e4a1f7d263`; additive). That device sees `held_elsewhere` and must choose **Continue here** (`POST /v1/attempts/{id}/continue-here`); the first device then has to do the same, so there is no silent tug-of-war. Each sends answer operations from one to three tabs with their own revision counters (so tabs collide), plus: duplicate retries; reordered batches; a reused `op_id` with a different payload; acknowledgements lost on the way back and re-sent; and database commit failures injected *inside the answer-save commit* (a failover; the request returns 503 and nothing is admitted). A plain model of the §10.5 rules predicts every disposition and the final answers. The server matched it exactly, and submission scored exactly the model's final answers. No defect was found. **Two checks on the test itself:** a mutation letting equal revisions overwrite is caught; and the first draft's injected failures were hitting the sign-in bookkeeping commit, not the save, so injection is now confined to `attempts.save`. Publication refuses any lesson, MCQ or written block that the oldest supported renderer can't show unless a reviewed fallback is attached (§5.4 rule 4). Forms are built only from published versions, so every test is renderable on every supported client by construction, and no per-request client filter is needed. `tests/test_client_compatibility.py` proves this for MCQs: a question with an equation and no fallback can't be published and never enters the practice pool. | MEDIA-UPLOAD-01 | 2026-10-11 | **Resumable uploads of lecture master videos (P07.S2.T1).** Storage is the private local archive (`PORTAL_ASSET_DIR/masters/{lecture}/{sha256}.{ext}`) until object storage and the media provider exist (B03, B05). When complete the session is committed `processing`, then the file is checked: video signature, and SHA-256 against the owner's record. The schema names were made distinct (`MediaUploadOut`/`MediaUploadIn`) after they clashed with the written-upload contract. | Reversible |

They are **pinned to the master's SHA-256**. Validation: storyboard order, the first scene at 0 s, strictly increasing, inside the master's duration. Replacing the master (upload or registration) keeps the old markers for reference but marks them for re-timing. Submission for review needs current markers, so every approved lecture has them. | CONCEPTS-01 | 2026-10-11 | **Learning concepts (outcomes) and prerequisites (P05.S1.T2).** `concept` (stable code, title, outcome statement, subject, source refs, status), `concept_topic` and `concept_prerequisite` (migration `e3b9f1c7a426`). A concept is written **once** and mapped to the topics that teach it in any edition or class, so a concept met in Class XI and revisited in Class XII is one record with two mappings, never a duplicate. Mapping a topic needs a content-author role in that topic's class and subject, and only the concept's own subject. Prerequisites form an **acyclic** graph: an edge that would close a cycle, including a self-edge, is refused (`PREREQUISITE_CYCLE`). An edge may name a live **bridging lesson** of the same subject. Concepts are staff-authored academic content, never generated here. A draft is published only by an **independent** subject reviewer (not its author), once it cites source pages and maps a topic; a published concept is frozen. Studio `/studio/concepts`: draft, cite pages, map topics book by book and chapter by chapter, prerequisites, and reviewer publishing. **Verified in a real browser** (headless Chromium, dev database, `[FIXTURE]` concepts): the full author and reviewer journey; axe clean. | Reversible |
| INCLUSION-01 | 2026-10-11 | **Inclusion records, mapping review, gaps and coverage (P05.S2.T1–T3).** `inclusion_record` (migration `f5c2a8d4b937`): for one published concept in one **independent mapping** (full curriculum, board syllabus, MDCAT, ECAT; board and entrance mappings per year), the state (included, excluded, not applicable, unverified), reason, source and **source version**. One record per concept, mapping and year. Records are drafted by an author and published only by an **independent** subject reviewer; a published record is frozen, and a change is a new year or source version. Reports list `unmapped` concepts (no published record in a mapping) and `contradictions` (an entrance or board syllabus including what the full curriculum excludes or marks not applicable). Coverage counts, for each mapped concept, its live lessons, live practice question families and academically approved lectures through the mapped topics. Content *available* is never shown as syllabus coverage *claimed*. **The learner's syllabus-coverage meter now reads these records:** included concepts for the learner's exam (else the board syllabus) and year that are taught in their class's books, and how many of them have a live lesson. It stays **unavailable** until verified records exist, so book headings are never treated as a syllabus. Studio `/studio/inclusion`: pick subject, mapping and year; coverage table, needs-attention list, records, a draft form and reviewer publishing. **Verified in a real browser** (headless Chromium, dev database, `[FIXTURE]` record): draft, publish, coverage updated; axe clean. Real mappings need verified syllabi (B02) and named reviewers (B01). | Reversible |
| BROWSERS-01 | 2026-10-11 | **Supported browsers and cross-browser journeys (P19.S2.T1).** Policy recorded in `docs/project/SUPPORTED_BROWSERS.md`: Chromium (Chrome, Edge, Samsung Internet, Opera, Android WebView) current and previous two majors; Firefox current and ESR; Safari 17+ / iOS 17+. CI now installs Chromium, Firefox and WebKit and runs `e2e/browsers.spec.ts` in three extra Playwright projects (`firefox`, `webkit`, `iphone`): browse to a chapter and read a preview lesson with its images, keyboard-only sign-up through the skip link, and a keyboard-only practice answer and submit. Chromium keeps the full suite with axe. **Known limits:** Safari moves focus to links with Tab only when the user turns that on, so the skip-link step is skipped for WebKit (every control stays reachable); WebKit and iPhone runs are engines and device profiles, not physical devices (B09); Playwright Firefox can't start on the development laptop (Windows side-by-side configuration error, not changed), so Firefox evidence is CI-only. *Evidence:* WebKit and iPhone journeys passed locally; all three in CI on the merged head. | Engineering (roadmap P19.S2.T1) | Physical reference devices (B09); owner review of the supported range against real learner traffic once analytics exist. |
| ISOLATION-01 | 2026-10-11 | **Privacy isolation (P19.S3.T3, second bullet): shared caches, shared devices, search and device markers. The first bullet (versioned report reconciliation) is NOT done.** **API:** every response now defaults to `Cache-Control: private, no-store` in the security-headers middleware; public catalogue routes keep their explicit `public, max-age`. A sweep built from the live OpenAPI document (every non-public GET without path parameters, as a learner and as staff holding every role) failed first on 9 routes that returned personal or staff data with no cache header (`/v1/exam-profiles`, `/v1/mock-sessions`, `/v1/admin/exam-profiles`, `/v1/ops/features`, `/v1/ops/signals`, `/v1/ops/written/auto-assessments`, `/v1/studio/block-registry`, `/v1/studio/imports`, `/v1/studio/syllabus-notices`). It now passes, and a new route is covered automatically. An attempt and its result are private and 404 for another learner. Search is private: the words searched for never reach the request log (path only), the audit trail or analytics, and a signed-in search answers exactly what an anonymous one does. A device another account used tells the next account nothing about that account (id, email or device id) in the claim decision, the device list or the access summary. **Web:** sign-out is a same-origin form POST to `/account/sign-out`, a route handler that replaces the `signOut` server action. It ends the API session, deletes the cookie and returns 303 with `Clear-Site-Data: "cache", "storage"`. Because it is a full page load, no page that Cache Components keeps hidden in the document survives; a client-side sign-out would have left recent pages in memory. The form also clears localStorage and sessionStorage itself for browsers that ignore the header. Unsent note drafts are keyed by a one-way hash of the account (`deviceScope`), so a session that ends without signing out (expiry, revoked elsewhere) never shows one person's draft to the next. **Mobile:** sign-out deletes unsent note drafts and unacknowledged answer queues, and drafts are filed per account (`accountScope`). **Trade-off:** signing out discards unsent drafts and unacknowledged practice answers on that device; the server keeps everything it acknowledged, and the account page says so. *Evidence:* `tests/test_privacy_isolation.py` (4: OpenAPI sweep, cross-account attempt and result, private search, shared-device disclosure) and the full API suite passed locally; `e2e/shared-device.spec.ts` (2) passed locally in Chromium against the dev servers. | Engineering (roadmap P19.S3.T3) | Versioned report reconciliation (scores, cumulative adjudications, trial-device authorizations, purchase credits, cohort snapshots; purchases and cohort reports are not built yet); mobile shared-device check on real devices (B07/B09/B13); Safari/iOS handling of `Clear-Site-Data`. |
| ROLLOUT-01 | 2026-10-11 | **Feature rollouts per environment (P16.S3.T1).** A feature can be released to a share of learners per environment. `ROLLOUTS` in `system/operations.py` holds each flag's default percentage per environment role; deployed roles without a default read 0%, and test follows development. An operator override (`feature_rollout` table, migration `a1d7e4b9c602` after `f5c2a8d4b937`) needs the operator permission, a multi-factor sign-in and a reason of at least 10 characters, and is audited (`ops.rollout_changed`, with the previous percentage). A learner is in or out by a stable hash of the flag and their account, so the answer never flickers; staff always see the feature, so it can be checked before learners get it. A flag only ever restricts: it hides the feature and its entry routes answer 404 `FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE`. It never bypasses a permission, scoring or publication rule, and data kept behind it is untouched. The first flag is `study_tools` (100% everywhere by default): the whole study-tools API router depends on it, and web (chapter page, dashboard, `/study`) and mobile (chapter, dashboard) read `GET /v1/me/features` (private, no-store). `GET /v1/ops/rollouts` is the runtime snapshot for a release record, and the release manifest carries the defaults (`feature_rollouts`). Operators change rollouts on `/admin/service`. The dev seed gains `studio-operator@example.com` (platform_operator, development only). *Evidence:* `tests/test_rollouts.py` and the targeted suites passed locally; migration round trip and release check clean on a scratch database; a local headless-Chromium check as operator set `study_tools` to 0% (a fresh learner lost the note buttons, `/study` showed the not-available notice, the dashboard link was gone), then back to 100% (note buttons returned); axe found no serious issues; no overflow at 375 px in dark mode. | Engineering (roadmap P16.S3.T1) | Owner choice of which future features ship behind a flag and their production percentages; mobile gates are NOT device-verified (B07/B09/B13). |
| MIGRATION-COMPAT-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Upgrade and rollback compatibility (P19.S3.T1).** The baseline is build `362db5b` (2026-10-10, schema `e8b2d6f4a193`), the same cut-off RELEASE-01 uses for its contract rule. `tests/test_upgrade_compatibility.py` adds four tests. (1) **Oldest supported mobile client:** `tests/compat/mobile_2026-10-10.json` holds the 57 operations the baseline mobile app calls and the 105 schemas it was built against, extracted from that commit by `tests/compat/extract_mobile_contract.py` (two dev-only sign-in routes are outside the published contract). The current API must keep each operation, add no required input, keep every field the app reads with the same type, never send null or unseen enum values where it didn't, and still accept the old request bodies. A mutation test proves the check catches a removed field, a new nullable field, a new required body field, a new required query parameter and a removed operation. (2) **Rollback without erasing data:** the strategy is to run the previous build against the current schema and never `alembic downgrade` data (`docs/runbooks/release-rollback.md`; switching a feature off with ROLLOUT-01 comes first). The test migrates scratch databases to the baseline and to head and requires that no table or column is dropped or retyped, that new columns on existing tables are nullable or defaulted, and that no new unique rule or narrowed check applies (a widened list of allowed values is accepted). (3) **Populated upgrade:** `tests/compat/baseline_2026-10-10.sql.gz` (488 KB) is data written by the baseline build itself: 137 of its own API tests run against an empty database, then dumped (325 accounts, 33 attempts, 30 written attempts, trials and device claims, notebooks, support, notifications, exports, audit). The test loads it at the baseline schema, upgrades to head, runs `alembic check`, requires every row count to be unchanged, reads every row through the current models, and signs baseline learners into the current API: `/v1/me`, access, their practice attempt, its result and progress all answer 200. All dump content is already public (`@example.com` fixtures, fixture passwords, the catalogue and source registry from `content/`). The current contract and schema pass all checks; nothing needed fixing. *Evidence:* the four tests and the release tests passed locally in 40 s. One earlier local run took 82 minutes across midnight; the cause was not established (the laptop may have slept), and the timed rerun took 40 s. | Engineering (roadmap P19.S3.T1) | A real device running the old app build (B07/B09/B13); executing a rollback on a deployment (B03); moving the baseline at each release. |
| DEGRADATION-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Provider outages (P19.S3.T2).** `tests/test_service_degradation.py` makes each provider fail. A failure must be explicit: a clear state, or a 503 with `Retry-After`. It must never be recorded as the provider's success, it must leave core records as they were, and a retry must work once the provider is back. The tests failed first on six real gaps, now fixed. (1) **Identity:** when the identity provider's signing keys couldn't be fetched, the API answered 401 ("session expired"), which made the mobile app sign the learner out. It now answers 503 `IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE` and creates no account. An unknown signing key is still 401, and portal sessions, which don't need the provider, keep working. (2) **Email:** any adapter value other than `none` was recorded as `sent` through the development outbox. An adapter the code doesn't implement is now `unavailable` with "nothing was sent". (3) **Scanner:** `SCANNER_UNAVAILABLE` now says when to retry (`Retry-After: 60`). (4) **Evidence store:** a missing or unwritable store was an unhandled 500. Disk errors are now 503 `STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE`, a half-written object is removed, and written uploads and support attachments roll back with nothing stored, no page rows, and a working retry. (5) **Assets:** a failed delivery-copy write left the master file on disk with no version row. The upload now rolls back and removes both, as a 503. (6) **Lecture master chunks:** a disk error was a 500. It is now a 503 that keeps the resume offset, so the same chunk can be sent again. (7) **Device attestation:** a provider exception was a 500 that rolled back the uncommitted claim, and a marker written just before a crash could later read as reuse. Provider errors now record `unknown` (`provider_error`, never clean, never the fallback) and give `verification_pending` on the same claim. The claim is committed as `MARK_PENDING` before the remote marker write; the account lock is then taken again and the claim re-read in case a parallel retry moved it. A failed write goes to `MARK_UNKNOWN`, and the same key recovers it to a grant even when the provider now reports the device as used. Releasing the lock lets parallel claims for one account interleave; a claim that finds a parallel grant on re-locking is closed without writing a marker and handled as a request for an account that already has the trial, so outcomes stay as before (one `granted`, the rest `device_authorized` or `device_limit`) and each device is marked once. The first full-suite run caught this regression in `test_simultaneous_claims_for_one_account_create_one_grant` before the fix. (8) **Payments:** no provider route exists (B06). The test pins that no payment, checkout or webhook route exists, that staff can't grant a `paid` entitlement, and that the integration shows `unavailable`. A shared `Unavailable` error (503 plus `Retry-After`) and `StorageUnavailable` live in `errors.py`. *Evidence:* 11 tests and the full API suite passed locally; the two concurrency tests passed six and three repeated runs. **Remaining:** device authorization for an extra device has no claim row, so a marker write that times out can still read as reuse on retry (it goes to review, never a wrong grant). Provider-specific adapters (S3, SES, DeviceCheck/Play Integrity, payments) will need their own outage tests when they exist (B03/B04/B06/B07/B08). | Engineering (roadmap P19.S3.T2) | Real provider adapters and their outage drills; restore and outage runbooks once hosting exists (B03). |

| PRODUCTS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Sellable products (P14.S1.T1).** A `product` is what a learner can buy: the whole platform, a subject package, an exam package or a bundle. Its definition (kind, scope, duration of 1 to 366 days, a disclosed finite written allowance and description) is kept apart from its price and sales channel. Lifecycle: `draft` (editable), then `active` (on sale, definition frozen), then `retired` (no longer sold). Retiring never touches entitlements already granted, and changing an active product means retiring it and defining a new one. Scope is checked against the live catalogue: grades 11 and 12 only, each subject needs a book in that class, exam codes need an exam profile, a bundle combines at least two items, and the platform product has an empty scope. `product_price` holds append-only versions per channel in integer minor units with an ISO 4217 currency. A future price waits for its start, a price can't start in the past, and a version can take a product off sale. The only channel is `web`, the D02 default of fixed-duration one-time web purchases; store subscriptions wait on D04. The one 30-day platform trial can't be defined as a product. `GET /v1/products` is public: active products with the web price on sale now and no staff fields (allowlisted in the authorization matrix; `public, max-age=60`). Finance admin routes require MFA and are audited (`product.created`, `updated`, `activated`, `price_set`, `retired`). The web page `/admin/products` shows and enters amounts with ISO 4217 exponents: `Intl` shows PKR with no decimals and would display 250050 paisa as "Rs 2,501", which this check caught. The dev seed gains `studio-finance@example.com` (development only). **Not done here:** no product or price exists; the owner sets them (D02). Package-scoped access checks, granting packages and checkout are P14.S1.T2, P14.S1.T3 and B06; until then a package can be defined and priced, but access stays platform-wide. *Evidence:* `tests/test_products.py` (2) with the authorization matrix, privacy sweep, upgrade compatibility and release tests, and the full API suite passed locally; the migration `c7e2f9a4b815` round trip and release check were clean; `e2e/products.spec.ts` (define, put on sale, price, axe, public listing) passed locally in Chromium; the page was checked at 375 px in dark mode with no overflow. | Engineering (roadmap P14.S1.T1) | Owner: real products and prices (D02); store channels (D04); payments (B06). |
| ENTITLEMENT-SCOPE-01 | 2026-10-12 | **A granted product opens exactly its scope (P14.S1.T2, partly).** `require_access` now takes what a start needs: class and subject pairs (practice, including multi-subject; review; written; linked written) or an exam pattern (mocks and mock readiness). An empty scope (the platform product, the free trial and every existing grant) covers everything. A subject package covers only its own pairs. An exam package covers only its exam patterns: an exam pass isn't a subject plan, and a subject plan isn't an exam pass. A refusal is 403 `NOT_IN_PLAN` naming what isn't included, for example "Class XI Physics". Premium lessons, their files and lesson completion follow the same rule through `entitled_for`, which also keeps the trial-device rule. Written reservations draw only on entitlements that cover the form, so a package's marking units fund only its own work; a learner with a current plan but no covering units now gets `ALLOWANCE_EXHAUSTED` instead of the misleading "your plan ended". Staff grants (`POST /v1/admin/entitlements`) can name an active product (`PRODUCT_NOT_ON_SALE` otherwise), and its scope is copied onto the grant, so later product changes never alter it. `EntitlementOut` gains `product_code` and `scope` (additive; the old-client contract check passes). Web and mobile plan cards say what the plan covers. A new test pins that a timed attempt started before a plan's normal end keeps saving and submits, while new starts are refused. **Not done:** refunds, purchase-credit reversal (§16.10) and checkout wait on payments (B06); no web screen for staff grants yet (API only). *Evidence:* `tests/test_entitlement_scope.py` (5: subject package, premium lessons, exam package, marking units, plan ends mid-attempt) failed first and now pass, with the access, lesson, mock, written and compatibility suites and the full API suite; a local headless-Chromium check showed a Biology XI package opening the premium Biology lesson and a Physics XI package leaving it locked, with the plan text naming the coverage. | Engineering (roadmap P14.S1.T2) | Payments, refunds and §16.10 credit reversal (B06); a staff grant screen. |
| PRICING-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Transparent pricing and owner-reviewed purchase terms (P14.S1.T3).** Purchase terms (taxes, renewal, buying during the free trial per §16.9 T5, refunds) are text the owner reviews, never written by the code. One finance person drafts a version in `commerce_terms` (there is one open draft, which only its author changes), and a different finance person publishes it (a database check and the API enforce two people). Publishing supersedes the previous version; every version is kept and audited. `GET /v1/commerce-terms` is public (allowlisted) and returns only the published version. The public `/pricing` page, linked from the home page and listed in the sitemap, shows each plan on sale with its full price in ISO 4217 minor units, validity, coverage and the written allowance, plus the published terms. When nothing is priced or no terms are published, it says so plainly. It always says buying isn't available yet, and there is no checkout or buy button (B06). The page doesn't hard-code renewal or conversion promises; those come only from the published terms. The finance page gains the terms editor. The dev seed gains `studio-finance2@example.com` (development only) so the two-person rule can be exercised locally and in CI. Migration `d9a3c6e1f472` follows `c7e2f9a4b815`. **Not done:** real terms and prices are the owner's (D02, D09); currency and tax treatment beyond the published wording, invoices and checkout wait on B06. *Evidence:* `tests/test_commerce_terms.py` with the product, authorization matrix, privacy and release tests, and the full API suite, passed locally; the migration round trip and release check were clean; `e2e/pricing.spec.ts` (two people draft and publish, a priced plan and the terms on `/pricing`, no buy button, axe) passed locally in Chromium after fixing a real axe finding (a `<p>` inside the terms `<dl>`); the page was checked at 375 px in dark mode with no overflow. | Engineering (roadmap P14.S1.T3) | Owner: terms wording and prices (D02, D09); payments (B06). |
| OFFLINE-PACKS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Controlled offline study packs (P13.S3.T1-T3, partly).** `GET /v1/me/study-packs/{chapter_id}?format=N` builds one chapter for one learner at request time; packs are never stored server-side and are `private, no-store`. Contents: free preview lessons for anyone. Premium lessons and practice are included only under a current plan covering the chapter's class and subject (ENTITLEMENT-SCOPE-01), with the trial-device rule. Practice is limited to practice-pool, live multiple-choice questions at their published version, with the released key and explanation (§5.4, AC14); mock-reserved, quarantined, retired and draft versions and written questions are never packed, and timed or mock forms never leave the server. A question needing a passage carries the passage's published version or is left out. A question with diagrams is left out, because its files are served only inside an attempt today. Lesson files are listed with SHA-256 and fetched from the lesson's own file route. The content is one canonical JSON string; the manifest carries its SHA-256, byte size, `pack_format_version` (1), `content_schema_version` and `expires_at`. Expiry is the covering plan's end, or 30 days for a preview-only pack. An app that reads only older formats gets 409 `PACK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED` ("update the app"). `GET .../status` tells the app whether a saved pack may be kept and whether it may still hold premium content. **Mobile:** "Save for offline" on the chapter screen downloads the pack and verifies the content and every file against their SHA-256 before keeping anything (staged, then moved). Packs are kept per account (ISOLATION-01), refreshed when online (a pack holding premium content is deleted when the plan no longer covers it), dropped at expiry, and deleted on sign-out. Offline, the chapter screen offers the saved copy; the offline screen shows lessons and an unscored self-check that sends and saves nothing. The Expo web preview deliberately doesn't store packs. **Not done:** offline progress and note sync with operation IDs (P13.S3.T2); packing question diagrams; secure offline video (provider-dependent, not a launch promise). *Evidence:* `tests/test_study_packs.py` (3) passed locally; a deliberate mutation that let mock-reserved questions into packs made it fail. The full API suite passed locally. Mobile is typechecked and linted only: **not device-verified** (B07/B09/B13). | Engineering (roadmap P13.S3) | Device verification; offline sync (P13.S3.T2); question diagrams in packs. |
| OFFLINE-SYNC-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Offline progress sync (P13.S3.T2).** Two replay defects were reproduced first. (1) A note save whose response was lost, sent again from a queue with its old base revision, got 409 `NOTE_CHANGED` with the learner's own text. Saving text identical to the stored note is now an idempotent "already saved" that writes nothing; a genuinely different edit from a stale copy is still a conflict. (2) Two completions of one lesson at once, such as a queued replay racing a tap, made the second insert hit `uq_lesson_completion` and returned 500. Completion now inserts with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and emits the `lesson.completed` event only for the insert that happened: one completion, one event. **Mobile:** completions and note saves made offline are queued per account with operation IDs (`progress-queue`). A newer change to the same lesson's completion or note replaces the older one. The queue is sent in order on sign-in and whenever the app returns to the foreground. It stops and keeps everything on offline, server or ended-session errors, and drops an operation that can no longer apply (a conflict, or a lesson no longer readable); the note's text stays as the lesson's draft so the learner chooses. The queue is cleared on sign-out. The screens say "saved on this device, sent when you're back online". Phones may stop apps in the background, so syncing while the app is closed is not promised. *Evidence:* `tests/test_offline_sync.py` (2) failed first and now pass, three runs in a row, with the study-tools and completion suites; the full API suite passed locally. Mobile is typechecked and linted only: **not device-verified**. | Engineering (roadmap P13.S3.T2) | Device verification (B07/B09/B13); background sync where the OS allows. |
