One flagged row stands after checking: P12.S3.T2 meets its rules. No code changed. | Engineering (records) | The 46 buildable tasks, in Section 18 order (P13.S1.T4 next). |
| CLIENTS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Supported app versions and build capability manifests (P13.S1.T4; §5.4 rules 3-5).** `client_version_policy` is append-only per platform (android, ios); the web app is always current. Raising a minimum is announced at once and enforced no sooner than 14 days later (`NOTICE_TOO_SHORT` otherwise). The enforcement moment may not fall inside a scheduled mock window (`IN_MOCK_WINDOW`), and a window scheduled over it later defers enforcement to that window's close. Lowering a minimum (a rollback) applies at once. Changes are operator-only, need MFA and are audited. `client_manifest` records each build's capability manifest (renderer and pack-format versions, block types, equation renderer, font set) once per platform and version; a different manifest for a registered build is refused. `GET /v1/clients/policy` (public, allowlisted) answers `ok`, `update_soon` (with the date) or `update_required`. Once a minimum is in effect, a request from a native app below it (`X-Portal-Platform`, `X-Portal-App-Version`) gets 426 `UPDATE_REQUIRED` with the security headers. The policy check, runtime configuration, sign-out and health checks are exempt. Apps that don't state their version (builds before this one) aren't judged. The policy is read at most once a minute per platform. **Mobile:** every request sends the platform and app version; at start the app shows a dated notice or an "update required" screen. **Web:** `/admin/service` gains "Supported app versions" (set a minimum, see registered manifests). Client schema names carry a `Client` prefix: a first draft's `ManifestIn` collided with the written-attempt schema of the same name, which the contract diff caught. **Not done:** publication validation still uses the code constant `SUPPORTED_RENDERER` rather than the oldest supported build's registered manifest; registering manifests from a release pipeline waits on B03; the minimum OS choice (D08) needs reference devices. *Evidence:* `tests/test_client_policy.py` (3) and the full API suite passed locally; a deliberate mutation removing the mock-window deferral made the tests fail; the migration round trip and release check were clean; a local headless-Chromium check as operator set a minimum (dated 14 days out), the public policy said `update_soon`, axe was clean, and the minimum was lowered back. The mobile notice and update screen are typechecked and linted only: **not device-verified**. | Engineering (roadmap P13.S1.T4) | D08 minimum OS (devices, B09); release-pipeline manifest registration (B03); drive publication validation from manifests. |
| BUDGET-01 | 2026-10-12 | **The §5.8 connection and background-work budget as checked data (P01.S4.T4, partly).** `portal_api/budget.py` lists every process class with its process count and per-process pool: api 20 × 10; critical 4 × 5; standard 4 × 3; bulk 2 × 3; reserve 7; written ingestion 2 × 3, reading 4 × 2, assessment 4 × 2 and regrade 1 × 2. That is core 245 plus written 24, or 269 planned, plus a budgeted rolling-deploy overlap of two API replicas (+20), for a peak of 289. Every installed database command is assigned to a class, and a test fails if a new command isn't. `portal-budget` prints the sheet and, given the funded capacity (`PORTAL_DB_SERVER_CONNECTIONS`), says whether the peak fits; the release manifest records the plan. `docs/project/CAPACITY_BUDGET.md` sets out the planning numbers and how each worker class sets `PORTAL_DB_POOL_SIZE`. It also records what the clients do today, read from the code: no periodic polling (the optional §5.8 clock check isn't implemented), 400 ms save debounce with one save in flight, and failed-save backoff from 2 s up to 15 s with jitter. A shared reconnect flushes every waiting client at once; that synchronized burst is the envelope P20 must measure, and 0 to 1 s reconnect jitter is the noted mitigation. The document also covers admission control as it exists and the payload budgets. **Not done:** these are planning numbers only. Measured envelopes, per-endpoint admission budgets and the funded server capacity wait on P20 and B03. *Evidence:* `tests/test_budget.py` (3) and the release tests passed locally. | Engineering (roadmap P01.S4.T4) | Measurement (P20, B03); per-endpoint admission budgets. |
| ITEM-STATS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Observed question statistics (P08.S4.T3).** For each published version of a multiple-choice question, the statistics come from finalised attempts (practice, mock and review) at each attempt's current score version, so corrections count. They are: observed difficulty (the share of answered responses that were correct) with blanks counted separately; the choice rate for each option, with the key marked; and discrimination as the point-biserial between this question and the rest of the attempt, taken as a share of its maximum so the question doesn't correlate with itself. Each figure carries its sample size. Responses scored under a void or credit-all correction are left out. Below 30 answered responses nothing is judged ("too few responses to judge"). From 30 up a question may be flagged *for a reviewer's look*: very hard (below 20 %), very easy (above 95 %), weak discrimination (below 0.15), a distractor chosen more than the key, or a distractor never chosen. Nothing is ever retired, edited or hidden automatically. Studio: a "How learners answered" panel on published questions, and a scoped "Questions worth a review" list on the academic overview (`GET /v1/studio/items/{id}/statistics`, `GET /v1/studio/statistics/flagged`, private). Finding: correct answers are scored with treatment `NONE`, not `NORMAL`; the first run caught it and the code uses the real values. *Evidence:* `tests/test_item_stats.py` (3: the maths against `statistics.correlation` on synthetic responses, flag thresholds and the minimum sample, and real fixture attempts with scope checks) and the full API suite passed locally; a local headless-Chromium check as the dev-seed reviewer showed real local data (one answered response: "too few responses to judge", discrimination undefined) with axe clean. | Engineering (roadmap P08.S4.T3) | Real responses at scale (launch); thresholds reviewed by the academic lead. |
| SEARCH-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Content search over published lessons (P11.S2.T3, partly).** `GET /v1/search` (moved unchanged into `content/search.py`) gains `lessons=true`: with it, published lessons are searched as well as chapters and topics; older app builds don't send it and get exactly what they got before. **Access at query time:** only a live item's published version of a learner-readable kind is searched; drafts, revisions in progress, quarantined and retired items, and questions never are. Anyone finds a live lesson by its *title* (titles are listed for everyone), shown locked. Lesson *text* is matched and quoted only where the caller may read it: free previews for everyone, premium lessons for a plan or trial covering that class and subject (the chapter page's rule, R07; a trial on an unauthorised device reads nothing). Opening a result goes to the lesson on its chapter page, which checks publication and access again. **What text:** `lesson_search_text(body)`, a database function (migration `f6a1c3e8b204`), takes headings, paragraphs, lists, callouts, tables, equation text alternatives, worked-example titles, problems and steps, diagram captions and descriptions, and handout titles. It leaves out checkpoints and worked-example answers, so a snippet never gives an answer away. It never raises (a malformed draft indexes as empty text instead of blocking a save), and a GIN index covers `to_tsvector('english', lesson_search_text(body))`. **Matching:** full-text search with English stemming (Urdu and Roman Urdu words match as written; Urdu tokenising needs a UTF-8 database locale, which a test checks), trigram similarity for typos in titles, and snippets from `ts_headline` returned as text runs with the matching words marked. **Alias table:** a search term group holds equivalent forms (English, Urdu script, Roman Urdu spellings) for one class and subject, with the owner material it comes from. Each form's script must match its language. An author in scope proposes a group; a subject reviewer in scope who didn't propose it approves it; reviewers retire groups with a reason (never deleted); all audited. Only approved groups widen a search: a form found in the query (whole words) is swapped for the others, and a whole query close to a form (trigram ≥ 0.7) brings in its group. The response lists the extra terms ("Also searched for"). Studio page `/studio/search-terms`. **Clients:** the web search page shows lessons with snippets or a locked note and gains a subject filter; the mobile search screen sends the session and shows lesson hits (typecheck and lint only; not device-verified). Search terms are still never logged or audited (ISOLATION-01). **Not done:** the alias table is empty until the owner supplies terms (B16): none of the owner's textbooks contain Urdu-script words (checked across all ten source texts); lecture transcripts aren't searched because no lecture is released to learners (B05); the multilingual benchmark is P11.S2.T4 (B16). *Evidence:* `tests/test_search.py` (4: access-aware lesson matching and snippets, publication states and questions, the two-person alias workflow and expansion, the text function and normalisation), the guard tests and the full API suite passed locally; Playwright `e2e/search.spec.ts` and `e2e/catalogue.spec.ts` passed locally (desktop and phone); a local headless-Chromium check covered guest and phone dark search and the propose, approve, widen and retire flow with a labelled fixture group (retired afterwards), axe clean. | Engineering (roadmap P11.S2.T3) | Owner terminology (B16); released transcripts (B05); benchmark (P11.S2.T4). |
| SETTINGS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Personal study settings (P04.S3.T2).** **Account:** `study_settings` (migration `a7d3e9f1c258`) keeps the learner's *usual* settings: theme (system, light, dark), text size (standard, large, larger), reduced motion, data saver, and lecture video quality (automatic, data saver, standard, high; for the player, B05). `GET /v1/me/settings` returns the defaults until something is saved; `PATCH /v1/me/settings` changes only the settings it names, so two devices changing different settings never undo each other. They are private, deleted with the account and in the personal data export. Study reminders stay in the notification preferences (NOTIF-01). **Device first:** each device keeps its own display and accessibility settings (theme, text size, reduced motion, data saver), and they win on that device. A device without its own takes the usual settings when the learner signs in (web: the sign-in action; mobile: the display provider), and a change made elsewhere never replaces a device's own choice. The system's settings are never overridden: "system" follows its dark mode, its reduced-motion setting applies even when the portal's is off, and system text size and browser zoom scale on top. **Web:** the device choice is a cookie with no personal data, so it survives sign-out's storage clearing (ISOLATION-01). A head script applies it before the first paint, and a small client sync re-applies it after in-app navigation. CSS gives `data-theme` (tokens and Tailwind's `dark:` variant), `data-text-size` (root font size 112.5 % or 125 %) and `data-reduce-motion`. With data saver (or the browser's `Save-Data` hint when the device hasn't chosen), lesson diagrams wait for a tap; question diagrams in tests always load. The account page has a "Display and data" section. **Mobile:** the device choice is stored in the app's private files; a display provider resolves the theme (and sets the native appearance), scales text, turns screen transitions off when the device or system asks for reduced motion, and holds lesson diagrams for a tap with data saver; there is a new "Display and data" screen (typecheck and lint only; not device-verified). *Evidence:* `tests/test_study_settings.py` (2) and the privacy export coverage test passed locally; Playwright `e2e/settings.spec.ts` (2) plus the accessibility, lessons and search specs passed locally. A local headless-Chromium check covered three simulated devices (choose and save; a new device adopting at sign-in; a device keeping its own light theme), reload before paint, sign-out keeping the choice, data-saver diagrams, 375 px and axe. It found and fixed a dropdown wider than its card at 375 px. | Engineering (roadmap P04.S3.T2) | Device verification (B07/B09/B13); the lecture player using video quality (B05). |
| SCAN-QUALITY-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Actionable quality checks on uploaded pages (W03.S2.T1).** The isolated evidence worker now checks each logical page (each photo, and each PDF page) with Pillow only. **Checks:** blur (the strongest denoised edge response, skipped on a near-blank page); glare (a share of the page blown out to near-white, skipped when the paper is already pure white, as in a clean scan); writing cut off at an edge (ink in a border band where the paper itself reaches the edge, so a dark table around the page doesn't count); and a page too small in the photo (the bright page region's long edge, which replaces the old whole-photo size warning; photos only). Blur and glare are measured on the page region at its own working size. Each check is `likely` or `possible` and carries a retake suggestion; `possible` asks the learner to check the preview. Nothing is ever rejected on quality, and nothing is called unreadable: a teacher decides that and can ask for a clearer copy through the existing rescan route (W04.S3.T2). **Stored and shown:** `written_page.quality` (migration `b8e4f2a6c931`); `PageOut.quality` gives code, level, sides and message to the learner and to reviewers; upload `warnings` stay as plain messages for older apps. The web written runner shows each page's checks under its preview, saying they are automatic checks, not a judgement, and how to use a clearer photo instead (a page not chosen for any answer isn't sent for marking). The marking workspace shows reviewers what the learner was warned about. The mobile written screen lists each page's checks (typecheck and lint only). **Provisional:** the thresholds were set on labelled synthetic pages (printed fixture lines, blur, glare, cut-off, small, on a dark table, blank, white scan, JPEG). They need calibrating on real learner scans in the pilot. *Evidence:* `tests/test_scan_quality.py` (13: each check flags only its own problem, wording and never rejecting, stored per page through the API) with `tests/test_evidence.py`, the written, rescan, release and upgrade-compatibility tests passed locally, and the full API suite passed in CI on the merged head (the local full run was stopped once CI passed); Playwright written journeys (5, including a new glare upload) passed locally; a local headless-Chromium check of the learner view with axe clean. The marking-workspace line was not browser-checked. | Engineering (roadmap W03.S2.T1) | Threshold calibration on real scans (pilot); device verification (B14). |
| FEEDBACK-EVIDENCE-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Feedback linked to scan evidence (W07.S1.T2).** A teacher's award for each criterion may name up to six places on the script: a page, and optionally a region given as x, y, width and height fractions of the upright page preview. Previews are rendered upright (EXIF applied), so one transform holds at any display size. The marking workspace offers the pages mapped to that part, whole or by ninths (top left … bottom right). **Grounding:** an anchor must point to a page this question's sealed answer was mapped to, and a region must lie inside the page with some area. Another question's or another script's page, an empty or out-of-page region, or too many places is refused with a reason. Anchors are stored with the award in the score version. **Learners:** each released criterion lists its places in plain words ("page 1, top left"). Pages are numbered as the learner uploaded them, and the web result draws the region over the page preview. A place whose page preview is no longer stored is still named, with "that scan is no longer available", and nothing is highlighted. A place that isn't a page of this attempt is never shown. The mobile result names the places in words. **Not done:** anchors on clearer copies (rescan revisions) and free-drawn regions; automatic assessment anchors stay behind its publication fence (B10). *Evidence:* `tests/test_feedback_evidence.py` (refusals, plain words, page numbering, a missing scan) with `tests/test_written_marking.py` passed locally; the full API suite passed in CI on the merged head (the local full run was stopped once CI passed). Playwright journey "feedback points to the place on the page it is about" passed locally. | Engineering (roadmap W07.S1.T2) | Device verification (B14); rescan-copy anchors. |
| NEXT-STEPS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Targeted next steps on written results (W07.S2.T1).** A scored question that lost marks gets next steps made only from approved content. **Focus:** each criterion that didn't earn full credit is quoted in the published rubric's own words, with that criterion's listed common mistakes (up to five). An alternative route the learner didn't take is left out once another route in its group earned credit. **Lessons:** the live lessons for the question's topic, or its chapter when the topic has none (up to three). This is the same rule as the MCQ review links, now one shared helper (`content/related.py`) used by both. With none published, the result says so ("No reviewed lesson is published for this topic yet") and links the chapter page for its outline and textbook pages. Nothing is generated, so assessment never becomes an open-ended tutor. A question with full marks, or one not scored, has no next steps. Web and mobile written results show them. *Evidence:* `tests/test_next_steps.py` (rubric wording, untaken routes left out, full marks, a live lesson linked, the no-lesson note) with `tests/test_review_lesson_links.py` (unchanged behaviour on the shared helper) passed locally, with the full API suite run in CI on the exact head before merging; the Playwright feedback journey now checks the next steps and a lesson link. | Engineering (roadmap W07.S2.T1) | Device verification (B14); real rubrics with misconceptions (B01). |
| STAFFING-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Admission by staffing, overload behaviour and truthful turnaround (W06.S3.T2).** Operators record four figures on a scope's review capacity, all or none (migration `c9f5a3b7d042`; nullable columns with no new check constraint, so the previous build's writes stay valid). They are productive reviewer hours a week; the fixed obligations taken out of them (calibration, adjudication, training); teacher minutes to mark one script; and the turnaround learners are promised. The automatic route is disabled (B10), so every script takes the full teacher time; nothing reduces demand until automatic marks pass WG10. **Admission:** fixed obligations come off first, then the work already owed (queued cases and started tests, at the minutes per script). A new written test starts only while what is left covers another script within the turnaround, on top of the existing case limit. Recheck and completion work is never refused, a started test can still be submitted when admission has closed, and nothing approves answers automatically to clear a backlog. **Truthful turnaround:** learners see about how many days marking takes for a test started now (owed work at today's productive minutes a day, rounded up), or a plain "Marking is full right now" notice that submitted scripts are kept and will be marked. Operators see the minutes available, owed and admissible, the estimate, and a form on the service page. *Evidence:* `tests/test_review_staffing.py` (validation, exactly one more script admitted after fixed and owed work, the refusal with the honest estimate, accepted work still submitted, fixed obligations shrinking room) with `tests/test_question_outcomes.py`, upgrade compatibility and release tests passed locally; the full API suite runs in CI. The figures are operator inputs, not measurements: real staffing comes with B01. | Engineering (roadmap W06.S3.T2) | Real staffing figures (B01); demand forecasting and escalation (W06.S3.T1). |
| TRANSFORMS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Conservative page transformations (W03.S1.T3).** Before submitting, a learner may turn a page in quarter turns, trim its edges, or ask for a clearer-contrast preview. **The original is never changed:** each change re-renders the page from the untouched original in the isolated evidence worker. The fresh preview and its quality checks are stored under a new key (the old preview is swept later as unreferenced), and the choices are recorded as `written_page.transform` (migration `d1a6b4c8e357`). They are fixed once the script is submitted. **Derived coordinates:** a trim is stored as the kept part of the upright original, and a trim entered on the page as shown is mapped onto the original first. Regions on a transformed page (feedback places, detail views) map back to the original with one function (`evidence.to_source`). The marker's detail view renders the page as shown by default, and "View the original photo" renders it untouched, so a marker can always check nothing was trimmed away; provenance headers say which. **Keeping marks:** turning is lossless (no resampling), clarity is a monotonic contrast stretch, and a trim must keep at least a third of each side of the original, so faint ink, pencil, small marks and lines at the edge survive. **Not done:** automatic deskew (small-angle straightening resamples the page and could blur small marks, so it is left out), free-drawn trims, and the mobile controls. *Evidence:* `tests/test_page_transforms.py` (the region mapping for each turn, the checks, every mark kept through turning and clarity, worker renders, the API journey with a composed trim, an over-trim refused, fixed after submission, the marker's as-shown and original views, and the original byte-for-byte) with the evidence, scan-quality, feedback-place, written, marking, release and upgrade-compatibility tests passed locally; the full API suite runs in CI. The Playwright journey "a page can be turned and clarified before submitting" (the preview's dimensions swap) passed locally; local headless Chromium with axe clean. | Engineering (roadmap W03.S1.T3) | Mobile controls; deskew; device verification (B14). |
| CALIBRATION-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Independent calibration of automatic proposals (W06.S1.T3).** Automatic marking stays off for learners (B10, WG10); calibration is how it could earn trust. **Evaluation subset:** a fixed share of sealed scripts (`written_evaluation_percent`, default 100 while only the development fixture assessor exists), chosen by a hash of the attempt id, so a script is always in or always out. **Blinded:** teachers never see a proposal (the marking workspace has no path to one), so a script's first released teacher decision on its initial case is an independent label. **Comparison:** each question's validated proposal for the same sealed evidence and rubric version is compared criterion by criterion with that decision (`written_calibration`, migration `e2b7c5d9f468`). Comparisons are recorded idempotently as agreed or open. **Adjudication:** an academic adjudicator in scope, with MFA, records for each disputed criterion whether the teacher, the proposal, both or neither applied the rubric correctly, with a reason (audited). This never changes the teacher's score version, the learner's result or the proposal. The studio Calibration page lists comparisons with the agreement rate; teachers are refused. **Not done:** disagreement discussion threads and periodic calibration rounds for reviewers (W06.S3.T3); real proposals wait for an approved provider (B10). *Evidence:* `tests/test_calibration.py` (blinded comparisons from fixture proposals, agreed and open rows, teachers, non-MFA and out-of-scope staff refused, verdicts per disputed criterion only, adjudicating twice refused, the learner's result unchanged, idempotent refresh, a deterministic subset) with the automatic, authorization, privacy, release and upgrade-compatibility tests passed locally; the full API suite runs in CI. Local headless Chromium: the adjudicator's empty state (automatic assessment off locally) with axe clean, and a reviewer refused. | Engineering (roadmap W06.S1.T3) | An approved provider (B10); reviewer calibration rounds (W06.S3.T3). |
| WRITTEN-SIGNALS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Written learning signals kept apart (W07.S2.T3).** Written practice records its own typed events in the event dictionary, inside the transaction that does the work: `written.started`, `written.submitted` (pages, answered and declared-unanswered parts), and `written.result_released` at each of the three places a result is released (teacher decision, completion or deadline resolution, rubric-correction carry forward). A released result keeps marks as units out of the units available on scored questions; partial credit is never turned into right or wrong. Pending and unavailable questions are counted apart and add no units. `evidence_rules_v2`, the MCQ progress report and the dashboard meters are defined over binary MCQ responses only, and a test confirms written work adds nothing to them. There is no public percentile, rank or leaderboard; a test fails if any route or schema field introduces one before a separate valid comparison policy exists. *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_signals.py` (the three events with units and counts, a pending question adding no units, MCQ evidence and progress unchanged by written work, no rank, percentile or leaderboard) with the analytics-event, marking, outcomes, recheck, rescan, adjudication and records tests passed locally; the full API suite runs in CI. | Engineering (roadmap W07.S2.T3) | A written comparison policy, if one is ever wanted. |
| PERMITS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Expiry and revocation of written permits (W08.S1.T3, roadmap §20.13.4).** **Ordinary expiry:** a test started before the plan ended may still be uploaded, mapped, submitted and marked under its pinned terms. Nothing rechecks the plan after the start, and the result is shown. A new test needs a current plan (`ACCESS_REQUIRED`). **Withdrawal (refund or fraud revocation):** revoking an entitlement now ends, in the same transaction, every *unsealed* written test that entitlement funded (found through its reservation in the allowance ledger). The test is expired with `end_reason = plan_withdrawn` (migration `f3c8d6e0a579`; nullable, so the previous build is unaffected), its reservation is RELEASED to its bucket, uploaded pages are kept, and the change is audited (`written.permit_withdrawn`). Further uploads are refused with `PERMIT_WITHDRAWN` and a plain reason, and web and mobile say why the test ended. **Accepted work is never touched:** sealed scripts keep their receipt and stay in the marking queue, their results and recheck rights continue, and nothing retained is erased. **Provisional:** whether a fraud revocation should also withhold marking of already-sealed work is an owner policy decision; the default keeps accepted work, as §20.13.4 reads. Refunds themselves wait for a payment provider (B06); today revocation is a finance action. *Evidence:* `tests/test_permits.py` (a test started before the plan ended is finished and marked, with a new start refused; a withdrawn plan ends the open test, keeps its page, records RESERVED then RELEASED and the audit event, refuses uploads, and still marks and shows the sealed script) with the access, admission, entitlement-scope, written, outcomes, release and upgrade-compatibility tests passed locally; the full API suite runs in CI. Local headless Chromium: the learner's "plan withdrawn" notice after a finance revocation, axe clean. | Engineering (roadmap W08.S1.T3) | Owner decision on fraud and sealed work; refunds (B06). |
| PROFILE-CHANGES-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Profile changes reach new mocks only (P05.S4.T3).** Verified that a newly published exam-profile version shapes new mocks, while a mock built earlier keeps the version it pinned: its deadline, marks, negative marking, invalid-item policy, year and frozen questions. A reviewed VOID correction stays a separate record: each form applies its own pinned policy (EXCLUDE or CREDIT_ALL), a finished attempt gets a new score version with the first one kept, an open attempt gets it at submission, and no deadline moves. **New rule:** a pattern must resolve against the catalogue. Each section's class and subject needs exactly one book edition with live chapters. None means the section has nothing to draw from; two (an old and a revised book both live) would silently mix editions. `profiles.catalogue_problems` names the class, subject and editions and is checked when a draft is saved (422 with `errors`), again at publication (409, because the catalogue can change after verification) and whenever a mock is built (409 `PATTERN_UNSUPPORTED` with `problems`). Mock readiness now returns `problems` (required field), and the web mocks page lists them. **Also:** a version for an older exam year can't replace a newer published one (409 naming both). Unknown years are refused by the request schema (422 at `body.year`), missing sources by the rules schema, unknown patterns with 404. **Provisional:** the older-year rule and the one-edition rule (no edition-specific sections yet; the catalogue has one edition per class and subject today). *Evidence:* `tests/test_profile_changes.py` (3 tests; the second edition is a labelled technical record committed inside the test and deleted afterwards) with the exam-profile, mock, mock-session, mock-deadline, mock-pool, form-replacement, entitlement-scope, MCQ-correction, authorization, privacy, catalogue and upgrade-compatibility tests passed locally; the full API suite runs in CI. Browser: not checked locally (no published pattern in the dev database); web and mobile typecheck. | Engineering (roadmap P05.S4.T3) | Official patterns (B02); edition-specific sections if two editions are ever live together. |
| DAYS-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Progress calculations, events and days (P12.S4.T3).** *Defect found:* the database runs in UTC, so between midnight and 05:00 in Pakistan a learner's "today" was still yesterday. The study plan accepted today as a future target and counted one day too many. Lesson-opened events were deduplicated per UTC day. Analytics days and weeks (repeat study, lesson visits, signup and trial cohorts) were cut at 05:00 Pakistan time. On the web, about 30 server-rendered pages formatted dates in the server's timezone (UTC in production), so they could show a different day from the phone app for the same record. *Fix:* `portal_api/days.py` (`learner_today` follows the learner's timezone preference, Asia/Karachi by default; `day_of`/`week_of` bucket in Pakistan time). It is used by the study plan, the lesson-opened event and the funnel, learning and business reports. Web and mobile share one display zone (`lib/dates.ts` `TIME_ZONE`, Asia/Karachi) and `daysFromToday` for date inputs. Date-only plan values format as UTC so they never shift. *Verified:* duplicate answers, a replayed submission and its `attempt.submitted` event count once; a resumed attempt counts once and only when submitted; unanswered questions aren't counted as answered; a VOID regrade changes progress only through the latest score version, with the first kept; the dashboard's recent results equal the progress report's, record for record (web and mobile both read these endpoints). **Provisional:** one display timezone for everyone (a learner abroad sees Pakistan times; notification texts still use their own preference). *Evidence:* `tests/test_progress_checks.py` (3 tests) with the funnel, learning, business, analytics-events, study-plan, dashboard, progress, progress-bucket, lesson-completion, report-query, notification, authorization and privacy tests passed locally; the full suite runs in CI. Local headless Chromium at 19:0x UTC (13 Oct in Pakistan): the API refuses 13 Oct as a future target, the plan starts "Tue 13 Oct", the default target uses the Pakistan day, axe clean. Mobile: typecheck only. | Engineering (roadmap P12.S4.T3) | Device check of mobile date display (B07). |
| TRIAL-MATRIX-01 | 2026-10-12 | **Trial acceptance matrix (P14.S8.T3).** `docs/project/TRIAL_ACCEPTANCE_MATRIX.md` covers every trial scenario (AC29–AC44, AC55, AC64–AC66). | PUB-01 | 2026-10-08 | **One publication per attempt at a time; pending work always has an owner; appeals follow each question (review OCT8-02/03/04; refines RECHECK-01 and ALLOC-01).** Reproduced on PostgreSQL before fixing: (02) a partial completion left the remaining pending question with no queued case; (03A) an open recheck pinned to V1 was stranded by an ordinary completion release (`RECHECK_TARGET_STALE`, no way forward); (03B) different cases locked different rows, so a completion could carry a stale award forward over a concurrent recheck; an adjudicator's expansion into a pending question did not supersede its completion case; (04) a completion that left Q1 unchanged removed Q1's unused appeal. **Rules now:** `decide` locks the attempt row, then the case row (the global lock order; `request_recheck` and seal also take the attempt row first) and carries everything outside the case's questions forward from the current released result under that lock. A lease records the released version the marker saw; if another publication has since changed this case's questions, the decision is a recoverable 409 `RESULT_CHANGED`. An open recheck whose target moved only outside its questions is rebased automatically and audited (`rebases`); if a disputed question changed, it returns 409 `RECHECK_TARGET_CHANGED` and an academic adjudicator rebases it with a reason. It is never abandoned or restarted. After every release, each pending question has exactly one queued completion case: questions resolved elsewhere leave older cases, and uncovered ones get a successor that keeps the original due time (`predecessor_id`). Appeal eligibility is per question: a window runs 14 days from the release that last changed that question's awarded units, closes once a recheck covering it targeted that release or later, and is untouched by releases that don't change it. `portal-written-repair` gives any already-stranded pending question a case (idempotent, audited; 0 found locally). *Evidence:* `tests/test_publication_integrity.py` (6, including a barrier-driven concurrent completion and recheck with no lost award and each question consumed once); `test_recheck.py` updated to the per-question rule; API 178, E2E 45 passed locally. | Engineering (review OCT8-02/03/04; §20.10) | Administrative regrades (W06.S2.T3) must use the same lock order and rebase path. |
| DBHOLD-01 | 2026-10-08 | **No pooled connection is held during evidence parsing, storage or integrity hashing (review OCT8-01; corrects EVIDENCE-01's claim).** EVIDENCE-01 said inspection and storage held no transaction. That was wrong. Upload read the attempt and then ran the parser inside that read transaction, and after its rollback it read `attempt.id` again, which opened a new transaction that stayed open through storage. Seal hashed evidence the same way. Reproduced with pool instrumentation and an event-paused parser or hash read: 1 connection was held during each pause. The same stale-object path had a correctness effect: the admission lock returned the earlier identity-map object without reloading it, so an upload could be **admitted after the upload window closed** (reproduced: 201 instead of 409). **Fix:** copy IDs, caps, usage and storage references into plain values, end the transaction, then parse, store and hash; `_lock` always reloads the locked row (`populate_existing`). Parser concurrency is bounded per process (4 child processes, 20 s queue wait, then a retryable 503 `SERVICE_BUSY`), independent of the database pool. *Evidence:* `tests/test_connection_holds.py` (0 connections held during paused parse and hash; an unrelated request served from a one-connection pool; late upload refused with stored objects removed; parser saturation 503). This is **not** claimed as the cause of the unexplained OperationalError (R08). | Engineering (review OCT8-01) | Measure upload-heavy mixed load on real infrastructure (P20). |
| REL-02 | 2026-10-08 | **Connection-pool saturation under load: reproduced, three causes fixed, original 503 still open (review R08, part 2).** A bounded mixed load ran against one local development API process (pool 10, no overflow, 5 s pool timeout). The mix was catalogue reads, typo searches, development sign-ups (argon2) and authenticated reads, sampling `pg_stat_activity` each second. **Findings:** (1) the search SQL takes 10-15 ms in isolation, so slow SQL is not the cause; (2) under load the pooled connections sat *idle in transaction*, held by request threads doing Python work, and at 60 users requests waited past the pool timeout; SQLAlchemy's `TimeoutError` was unmapped, giving unhandled **500s** and dropped connections, not 503s; (3) a **cold-start race**: `get_engine` used `lru_cache`, so concurrent first requests each built an engine with its own pool. On the previous code a 16-thread cold start built 16 engines, and under load 49 backends were seen against a budget of 10. **Fixes:** a lock-guarded engine and sessionmaker per process (`tests/test_engine_singleton.py`); pool saturation answered as a logged **503 SERVICE_BUSY with Retry-After** instead of a 500; the development sign-up adapter hashes and verifies passwords with no connection held; the authentication dependency ends its transaction before the endpoint runs. **Measured after (same machine, one dev process):** cold start holds 10 connections; 20 users: 0 errors; 60 users: 0 500s and 0 transport errors, with honest 503s on about 10% of anonymous reads (overload of a single development process). **Still open:** the original intermittent E2E search 503 is a driver `OperationalError` class, which these runs never produced; the handler logs its class and correlation ID. None of this is capacity evidence: production capacity needs the B03 environment, multiple workers, the CDN and P20 load qualification. | Engineering (review R08; roadmap §5.8) | P20 load qualification on real infrastructure; release connections earlier on public read endpoints if measurements there show the same hold pattern. |
| TRIAL-02 | 2026-10-08 | **Trial claims, device authorization and recovery contracts (review R06; roadmap §16.2-16.7). Same-device protection is NOT complete.** Built: a durable `trial_claim`, written before any marker, with states PENDING_VERIFICATION, MARK_PENDING, MARK_UNKNOWN, MARK_CONFIRMED, GRANTED, REVIEW_REQUIRED and CLOSED_INELIGIBLE and a transition log. Retries with the same key recover the same claim, a lost marker response is re-attempted on the original claim, and after the 7-day window unresolved claims go to review. `trial_device_use` keeps the account grant separate from each installation's authorization; limits (proposed D07) are 3 devices with 1 self-service replacement per 7 days. Support reviewers issue audited, time-bounded shared-device exceptions (MFA). The §16.2 order runs under an account/program advisory lock: independent paid or other access is honored first; an existing grant keeps its dates and a new native device needs its own authorization (a web grant does not unlock a consumed native device, T2); prior paid history means no trial (T8); a consumed device means no automatic repeat; unknown evidence is never treated as clean or consumed. Protected starts and premium lessons check the native device rule on the server. **Evidence:** a provider interface with Apple DeviceCheck/App Attest and Google Play Integrity + device recall slots that return *unknown, provider not configured* (B07, B08), and a deterministic fixture adapter accepted only in development/test. **Mode:** `trial_device_evidence` is `required` (unknown never completes a native claim) or `fallback` (native activation proceeds on account history while providers are unconfigured, recorded on the claim and device as `provider_unconfigured`/`fallback`). Deployed roles must set it explicitly, plus a secret pepper for installation references. **Honest limits:** installation tokens are random per install, not device identifiers, and don't survive reinstall; until per-request attestation exists, the client type and install token are asserted by the app, so the native gate is cooperative. | ALLOC-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Per-question outcomes and allowance allocations; funded review capacity (review R05).** Consumption was whole-script at the first release, with no pending or unavailable outcomes and no capacity admission. Now: **(1) Ledger.** At seal each answered question gets its own ACCEPTED event with its weight; declared-unanswered questions accept nothing. A question is CONSUMED once, when it is first released as scored, and RELEASED (unconsumed units returned) when it is resolved unavailable. Remedy credits are separate REMEDY_CREDIT events, idempotent by their own key, with the defect reference, reason and granting user (finance or owner, MFA, audited). Pre-R05 attempts keep their attempt-level events and behave as before. **(2) Outcomes.** A marking decision sets each question in scope to scored, pending (with a reason, e.g. unreadable) or unavailable (with a reason). Pending and unavailable questions take no awards, so no zero is invented. Each score version records `question_status`, `completeness` (complete, partial_pending, partial_unavailable) and the maximum of the scored questions. Releasing with pending questions opens a **completion** case for exactly those questions, and it carries the rest forward. The learner sees no final total while anything is unresolved, and recheck eligibility covers scored questions only. **(3) Capacity.** A reviewer role grant is not funded capacity. Operators set `max_open_cases` per class and subject (MFA, audited). New written starts are admitted only while queued cases plus open permits in that scope are below the limit, decided under a scope-level lock. Accepted work (rechecks, completion cases) is never refused by capacity. Every case gets `due_at` (proposed 48 h, §20.10), and `GET /v1/ops/written-backlog` shows load and overdue cases. No capacity is invented by the migration; the dev seed sets a dev-only value. *Evidence:* `tests/test_question_outcomes.py` (6: only answered questions accepted; pending without awards, completion case, each question consumed once; unavailable returns allowance; rechecks can't defer; remedy authorisation, idempotency and conflict; capacity admission and restoration); E2E pending-then-completion journey; full suite. | Engineering (review R05; §20.9.3, §20.10, §20.13.3/4, W08) | Learner action on pending questions (rescan/confirm, W06.S2.T4) and the 7-day finite remedy and escalation automation; capacity forecasting (W06.S3.T1). |
| ACCESS-02 | 2026-10-08 | **Lessons are premium unless explicitly marked as free previews (review R07; corrects ACCESS-01).** ACCESS-01 called every published lesson free and the lessons endpoint served full bodies to anyone with a public cache header. The owner's model is a 30-day trial followed by a subscription, and the roadmap separates free previews from premium content, so that default was wrong. Now every content item has an `access_tier` (`premium` by default; migration `c7a1e2f4b9d0` sets every existing item to premium). A publisher in scope, with MFA, can mark a lesson as a `preview`, with a recorded reason (audited `content.access_tier_changed`). The lessons endpoint lists every live lesson; premium bodies go only to callers with an active entitlement (plan or trial), and others get the title with `locked: true` and no body. **Caching:** an anonymous response holds preview bodies only and may be cached publicly; any response to a request with credentials is `private, no-store`; both carry `Vary: Authorization`. | ADMIT-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Written permit admission is serialized per account (review R03).** Reproduced on PostgreSQL on 2026-10-08: five simultaneous starts for different forms all succeeded against a two-permit cap, because the open count was read before any lock and `reserve` trusted it. A permit past its upload cutoff also still counted as open until the background expiry ran. Now `start` takes a transaction-scoped advisory lock keyed by the account, then re-checks an existing attempt for the form, reads the database clock (the authoritative start, after any wait), locks the account's active attempts, expires those past U by the normal rules (releasing their reservations), counts the rest, and reserves under the entitlement lock. A refused start keeps those expiries and reserves nothing. *Evidence:* `tests/test_permit_admission.py` (5 simultaneous starts, 3 rounds: exactly 2 admitted and 2 reservations; 4 simultaneous starts of one form: 1 attempt, 1 reservation; a closed permit no longer blocks; start time read after a held admission lock), passed in 5 further repeated runs. | Engineering (review R03; §20.13.4) | Per-question allocations and remedies (R05). |
| EVIDENCE-01 (connection-hold claim superseded by DBHOLD-01) | 2026-10-08 | **Upload evidence is parsed, bounded and disarmed in a separate worker; pages are logical (review R01/R02).** The earlier validator trusted signatures and raw text patterns. Reproduced on 2026-10-08: a 29-byte PNG header and a 21-byte non-PDF were accepted, and a valid PDF using compressed object streams was rejected. Now `written/evidence.py` runs as a child process (`python -I -m`, minimal environment, 30 s timeout; on Linux also 1 GiB address space and 25 s CPU). It fully decodes with maintained parsers: **Pillow** for JPEG/PNG (60 MP decoded cap, 12,000 px per side, no animation, EXIF orientation applied) and **PDFium via pypdfium2** for PDF (encrypted, unparsable, more than 10 pages, document JavaScript or attachments refused). PyMuPDF was not used because of its AGPL licence. Each logical page gets a **freshly encoded PNG preview** (long edge 2,000 px, no metadata); learners and teachers only ever see previews, and originals stay private and immutable. Rasterising is the content disarm: active PDF content is never executed or served, so safety does not depend on a blocklist. **Logical pages:** `written_file` holds originals; `written_page` is one row per photo or PDF page with a stable id, and mapping, receipts and the 10-page cap count these. Separate caps: 10 files, 10 pages, 60 MB. **Locks:** inspection and storage happen with no transaction or lock held; the attempt lock covers only the clock, expiry, status, duplicate and cap re-checks and the inserts. A refused or failed admission deletes the objects it stored; `portal-written-sweep-orphans` removes unreferenced objects older than an hour (crash recovery). Seal verifies the original and the exact preview of every mapped page and pins both hashes in the receipt. Existing rows kept their ids as page 1 (migration `648941e093b0`); `portal-written-previews` backfilled 30 dev pages. Validation failure is a recoverable processing error, never a mark. *Evidence:* `tests/test_evidence.py` (19: the four review cases, truncated JPEG, decompression bomb, encrypted/JavaScript/attachment PDFs, 11 pages, oversize, metadata stripped, worker timeout, PDF pages counted against the cap, file and byte limits, cleanup after a refused admission, orphan sweep, per-PDF-page mapping and pinned previews, tampered preview); E2E PDF journey. Test fixtures now use real encoders: the old PNG fixture was itself header-only and passed only because of R01. | Engineering (review R01/R02; §20.8; OWASP file upload guidance) | Windows dev hosts get the timeout and decoder limits but no OS memory cap; deployed roles run Linux (B03). Object storage adapter (B03), malware scanning service if required by the hosting review. |
| RECHECK-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Recheck eligibility, scope and duplicates (corrects IMPL-23 after review R04).** A request targets the **current released score version** and must be made within **14 days of that version's release** (§20.10). It is stored structurally in `written_recheck_request`: target version, question positions, optional disputed criteria, reason, requester. The database allows **one request per target version**, so duplicate or concurrent requests open at most one case (a concurrent loser gets 409). Against a first result every question is eligible. Against a corrected result only the questions whose **awarded marks** that correction changed are eligible: unchanged questions already had an independent second look, and reworded feedback alone opens nothing. This keeps appeals against later corrections (§20.10.3) without allowing endless repeats. The recheck teacher re-marks **only the requested questions**; every other question's awards are carried forward exactly from the target version. Awards outside the scope are refused. Widening the scope needs the `adjudicate` permission in scope and a recorded reason. If the released result changed after the request (for example a regrade), the decision is refused as stale and needs academic review. Independent marker, no second allowance charge, CAS and full history are unchanged. Existing recheck cases were pinned by migration `1bc0b73fea5b` from their recorded question prefix; anything unparsable is reported, never guessed. *Evidence:* `tests/test_recheck.py` (4: scoped re-mark with carry-forward, appeal of a correction, duplicate refusal, original marker refused, adjudicator widening with and without reason, four concurrent requests open exactly one case, window from the disputed release); E2E written journey with a second fixture reviewer. | Engineering (roadmap §20.10, W06.S2.T1/T2; review R04) | Regrade/adjudication cases (W06.S2.T3) and rescan classification (W06.S2.T4) create their own targets. |
| REL-01 | 2026-10-08 | **Post-registration 401 explained and fixed (review R08, part 1).** The development identity adapter created its ephemeral RSA issuer through `lru_cache`, which doesn't stop concurrent first calls from each generating a key. The cache kept one; tokens signed with the other failed verification, so a sign-up right after an API start could get 401 from `POST /v1/sessions`. Reproduced deterministically (8 threads, `Signature verification failed`) and fixed with a lock-guarded per-process issuer (`tests/test_dev_issuer_race.py`). The deployed OIDC path verifies with JWKS and has no per-process signing key; its client cache can at worst fetch the same keys twice. **The intermittent search 503 stays open**: it has not recurred since DB-error logging was added and has no root cause yet. Lower E2E worker counts are a CI resource choice, not capacity evidence. | Engineering (review R08) | Bounded mixed-load reproduction for the 503 with pool, lock-wait and CPU measurements. |
| IMPL-23 | 2026-10-07 | **Support requests, academic reports and written rechecks (P15.S3, W06.S2.T1).** Learners open help requests in five categories (account, plan/trial, technical, question report, other), with at most 5 open at once, and reply in a thread. A *question report* is opened from a result: the reference (an attempt the learner owns plus a position) is resolved on the server to the exact question item and **frozen version** the learner saw, never trusted from the client. **Routing and privacy:** support staff (`view_support_context`) see every request with minimal context (email, trial state, whether access is active), and each view is audited as `support.context_viewed`. Subject reviewers see only question reports in their grade/subject scope (filtered in SQL before the limit) and never the learner's identity. Staff replies can be **internal notes**, which learners never receive. Status moves open, in progress, waiting for the learner, resolved. **Rechecks:** once a written result is released the learner may ask once, within **14 days**, naming the questions and a reason. That opens a recheck case on the same sealed evidence and **consumes no allowance**. The API refuses the recheck lease to any teacher who marked an earlier version of that script, so a recheck is always an independent second look. Every released version stays in the learner's history; a recheck never erases the first result. Reply boxes keep any text typed while a previous message was sending. *Evidence:* `tests/test_support.py` (ownership, open limit, scoped routing without identity, internal notes hidden, recheck history, original marker refused, no double consumption, window closes after 15 days); E2E `support.spec.ts` (help thread with internal note and status, question report to a reviewer without identity, staff-only) and the extended written marking journey (recheck request reaches the marking queue). Full suite 41 passed, 0 failed. | Engineering (roadmap P15.S3, W06.S2.T1) | Notifications for replies (P15.S1), SLA timers and assignment, linking a question report to adjudication (P10.S3.T4), regrade/rescan cases (W06.S2.T3/T4). |

| IMPL-22 | 2026-10-07 | **Trial, entitlements and the written allowance ledger (§16, P14.S5, §20.13.4).** One `PLATFORM_TRIAL_30D` grant per account (unique per program; terms revisions never reset it). It starts at the grant's commit time and ends exactly 30 x 24 h later; repeat activations return the original dates, and prior paid history means no trial (T8). Every access source is an `entitlement` with provenance (trial, paid, scholarship, promotional, pilot), resolved per request. Revocations and refunds are recorded states, never deletions. Staff grants and revocations need `grant_entitlements` (owner_admin, finance) with MFA and are audited. **Allowance ledger** (append-only, idempotent per attempt and kind): RESERVED at written start, atomically with the attempt and funded from the bucket that expires first under a row lock; ACCEPTED at seal for questions with submitted work, with the rest released; CONSUMED once at the first released marks (draft marks don't consume); RELEASED on unsealed expiry. **Device recognition** (DeviceCheck/App Attest/Play Integrity recall) isn't available (B07/B08). The decision uses account history only, records `device_check: unavailable`, and claims nothing more. Web and mobile show the plan, trial button, allowance and an honest ended or not-eligible state. **Found and fixed:** the Expo web client's trial request sent `X-Portal-Client`, which CORS didn't allow (regression test added); 503 responses now log the database error class and correlation id. *Evidence:* `tests/test_access.py` (7) and practice E2E with the trial started through the UI; mobile checked on the Expo web target ("Free trial until 6 November 2026"). | Engineering (P14.S1/S5/S7, W08.S1) | Web payments (B06), store billing and device recall (B07/B08), claim recovery window (T3), conversion credit (T5). |
| IMPL-21 | 2026-10-07 | **Teacher marking of written scripts (W06.S1, §20.10) and scoped queues in SQL.** Sealing opens one initial review case in the same transaction. The marking queue is scoped by the reviewer's grade and subject grants, and shows a short script reference, never the learner's identity. Reviewers can't mark their own work. A reviewer takes a 20-minute lease, and decisions carry the case's expected version (CAS): a second teacher can't take a held lease, and a stale decision gets 409 with the current version. Awards must be permitted rubric levels; parts the learner declared unanswered score 0; a dependent criterion can't earn credit when its prerequisite earned none; and only one route per alternative group is credited. Each decision creates an immutable `written_score_version` recording the actual assessor, case kind, rubric versions, evidence epoch (receipt), per-criterion units and reasons, totals and prior version. Learners see marks only after release; a saved draft mark is never a result. **Performance fix found in E2E:** the content and marking queues checked scope per item after the LIMIT (N+1 queries, and in-scope items crowded out). Scope is now one grant query turned into a SQL filter applied before the LIMIT, with related rows batch-loaded. The development queue fell from about 1.0 s to about 0.3 s, and a regression test covers scope before limit. *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_marking.py` (5) and the web E2E loop: learner submits, reviewer fixture marks with a reason and releases, learner sees 1/5 with the reason. Full E2E 74 passed and 0 failed over 2 repetitions. | Engineering (§20.10, W06/W07) | Recheck requests (W06.S2.T1), rubric adjudication regrades (W06.S2.T3), rescan classification (W06.S2.T4), staffing forecasts (W06.S3). |
| IMPL-20 | 2026-10-07 | **Written practice attempts (W03/W04, §20.7.2).** Forms are offered only from live written questions with a published rubric for their exact version, and only when a teacher reviewer is staffed for the class and subject. The timing triple D, G and U = D + G is pinned at start; G is 10 minutes for chapter practice and is disclosed before start, and untimed practice gets a 24 h upload permit. The UI says plainly that home writing time can't be enforced. **Evidence:** raw bounded uploads (no multipart dependency); signature-based JPEG/PNG/PDF checks; 15 MB per image and 40 MB per PDF, 10 files and 60 MB per script; header-only dimensions; PDF page cap; refusal of active/encrypted/embedded PDF content. The server computes SHA-256 and stores immutable private objects. Exact duplicates are reused only within the same attempt, and only the owner can retrieve a page. **Mapping:** slot → pages or an explicit unanswered declaration, with revision concurrency. **Seal:** hashes are re-verified outside the lock; then lock, take the clock after the lock, require admission at or before U, check revision and completeness, and write one receipt holding a manifest snapshot and page hashes. Exact retries return it even after U, a changed payload conflicts, and post-seal edits are refused. Unsealed attempts expire after U and are never partially sealed; received pages are listed honestly. **Web:** uploads go through a same-origin route handler that checks the Origin and attaches the session server-side. Progress is shown per file, and the states local, uploading, uploaded-not-submitted and submitted are kept distinct. Deployed roles refuse the local evidence directory until object storage exists (B03). *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_attempts.py` (9, including tamper detection and cutoff admission) and the web E2E written journey plus the cross-site refusal; full suite 72 passed and 0 failed over 2 repetitions. | IMPL-19 | 2026-10-07 | **Written questions and rubrics as reviewed content kinds (§20.6).** `written` holds the stem, subparts and maxima in **integer hundredths** (100 = 1 mark), answer language, expected structures and origin. `rubric` is its own item attached to one written question (`parent_item_id`) and names the exact question version it marks. It carries its authority (an official scheme only with a published reference, otherwise a labelled practice rubric), the permitted increment (25/50/100), criteria with permitted award levels (must include 0 and the maximum, all multiples of the increment), dependencies, alternative-route groups (each counted once, at its largest maximum), expected concepts and the consequential-error, units and crossed-out rules. Submission requires the criteria to **reconcile exactly** with each subpart and the total. A rubric can be published only for an approved question version, and a written question only after a published rubric for that same version exists. Both kinds have their own reviewer checklists and are never learner-readable through content APIs. The studio has editors for both, with a live reconciliation table. *Evidence:* `tests/test_written_records.py` (11) and the studio E2E written journey. Automatic reading or grading is out of scope until provider approval and qualification (W02/W09). | Engineering (§20.6, W01) | Written forms with D/G/U timing, private scan upload and mapping, sealed receipts (W03/W04), then teacher review (W06). |

Pending operations persist in browser storage, are shown as pending (never saved), and are re-sent after a reload, a closed tab or a dropped connection; exact replays return their original receipts. Saves are debounced at 400 ms. The countdown uses a server-clock offset measured after mount, for display only. Before the deadline: a best-effort pre-flush at D−5 s, editing closes at D, queued revisions are still sent, and the server's expiry is picked up after C. Submit shows answered, unanswered and pending counts, sends the pending operations with a persisted idempotency key so retries reuse it, then replaces history so Back never re-enters a submitted test. | IMPL-17 | 2026-10-07 | **Attempt protocol as specified in §10.5.** Every write locks the attempt row and reads `clock_timestamp()` **after** the lock as `admitted_at`. That locked time alone decides admission against C = D + T (T pinned at start: 3,000 ms for self-paced timed practice, 0 for future ranked mocks). Client, proxy or API arrival never counts. Each client operation is recorded once (`answer_op`, unique per attempt and op_id) with a disposition: accepted, stale, invalid, late, finalised or locked. A reused op_id with a different payload is a *conflict* and is not stored. Exact replays return the stored receipt, even after finalisation. A genuinely new write to a finalised attempt gets **409 ATTEMPT_FINALISED** with the receipt id. Manual submit applies the pending operations (including the last debounced click) and finalises once, in one locked transaction, writing a single `submission_receipt` with a ledger hash. Repeats return the same receipt; operations presented later appear in a separate reconciliation list, and the receipt is never rewritten. Expiry happens only when the locked clock is past C: lazily on any read or write, and by `portal-expire-attempts` (SKIP LOCKED). A manual submit admitted after C adds nothing and is recorded as expiry. **Forms** sample published live questions, one per canonical family, with a stored seed, and freeze versions, option permutations, marks, timing and the scoring policy. A short pool fails honestly with the available count. **Scoring** is a pure function: EXCLUDE, CREDIT_ALL and KEY_CORRECTION overlays, half-up percentages, NOT_SCORABLE at zero maximum, and `score_version` unique on its effective inputs. *Evidence:* `tests/test_attempts.py` (14), including an 8-iteration save-versus-submit race. An accepted save is always in the receipt ledger; a losing save is reported as finalised and never dropped. | Engineering (roadmap §10.5, P09.S3, P10.S1 to S3) | Adjudication records and regrade jobs (P10.S3.T4), scheduled ranked mocks (P09.S2), the learner web/mobile attempt UI with durable local queues (P10.S2.T2) follow. |
| IMPL-16 | 2026-10-07 | **Questions reuse the editorial workflow as content kind `mcq`.** One workflow serves every academic item kind (`kinds.py`). Each kind supplies its schema, submission and publication validation, reviewer checklist, quarantine levels and learner readability. This avoids a second, divergent approval path. **MCQ v1 (P08.S1/S2):** stem, 2–6 options with **stable option IDs** (never renumbered), one correct option, marks, shuffle flag, explanation (correct reasoning, per-distractor notes, worked steps), editorial metadata (kept separate from later empirical statistics), origin label (`original_practice` or `authorised_past_paper` with an authorisation reference), language, and source pages. Drafts may be incomplete. Submission requires a complete question with a finished explanation, unique option IDs and text, a valid key and consistent past-paper data. Options that refer to other options warn against shuffling. Approving a question requires the reviewer to confirm **accuracy, ambiguity, units, diagrams, grammar and mapping**, stored with the decision. Quarantine requires a §5.7 level (SOFT/VOID/KEY_ERROR). Variants share a canonical `family_id`, restricted to the same class and subject. **Key isolation (P08.S2.T3):** questions are never learner-readable through content APIs. The lessons endpoint filters to learner-readable kinds, and exam payloads will come from frozen form snapshots that omit keys. Studio previews show the key to staff only. *Evidence:* `tests/test_mcq_items.py` (10), and the studio E2E question journey (approve stays disabled until every check is ticked). | Engineering (roadmap P08, §5.7) | Adjudication records for VOID/KEY_ERROR, duplicate/near-duplicate detection, pool sufficiency and statistics follow with forms and attempts (P09/P10). |
| IMPL-15 | 2026-10-07 | **Staff studio UI.** `/studio` (queue), `/studio/new` and `/studio/items/{id}` run on the web BFF. Every mutation is a Server Action that forwards the staff session, so the API stays the only authority. Autosave is debounced and **chained**: a save waits for the previous one and uses the last acknowledged revision, so an editor never conflicts with their own in-flight save. A 409 stops autosave and shows a block-by-block comparison with the other editor. The editor then keeps theirs or saves on top. Nothing is silently overwritten. Unsaved edits are mirrored to browser storage and offered back on the next visit for the same base revision. Blockers from the API (no eligible reviewer B01, rights unverified, renderer fallback) appear beside the actions. **Development only:** the dev sign-in can simulate an MFA session, and `portal-dev-seed-staff` creates scoped Class XI Biology fixtures (refused unless role is development/test with the dev adapter). CI seeds them for the studio journeys. *Evidence:* `apps/web/e2e/studio.spec.ts` (staff-only access; draft, autosave, preview, submit; independent approval; no MFA means no publish control; MFA publish blocked by the rights gate; concurrent edits produce a conflict, never an overwrite). Full suite 56 passed and 0 failed over 2 repetitions with workers capped at 3 (CI 2). | Engineering (P06.S1) | Preview across mobile and fallback surfaces (P06.S3.T1), import UI, and topic/chapter catalogue editing follow. |
| IMPL-14 | 2026-10-07 | **Editorial workflow engine (P06).** A `content_item` is a stable identity tied to a chapter (optionally a topic). Its text lives in numbered `content_version`s. Editorial state (draft → submitted → changes_requested/approved → published) is separate from learner **availability** (unpublished, live, quarantined, retired), so a correction can be drafted while the old version stays live or quarantined. **Rules enforced on the server:** (1) authors, reviewers and publishers act only inside the grade/subject `scope` of their role grant, and items outside it return 404; (2) **independent review**: anyone who edited a version (`contributors`) can't approve or publish it; (3) autosave sends the revision it started from, and a stale save gets **409** with the current text and editor; (4) submission needs schema-valid blocks and at least one source page reference to the chapter's **own textbook** (pages range-checked, with warnings for pages outside the chapter or recorded as missing); (5) publication needs an approval, an MFA session, renderer compatibility (§5.4 block registry: equations need a reviewed fallback until a typeset renderer ships) and **CONFIRMED publication rights** for every referenced source; (6) revisions need a reason, publishing one supersedes the previous version, and nothing is deleted (retired items are hidden). Every transition is audited in the same transaction. Source documents carry `publication_rights` (UNVERIFIED/CONFIRMED/DENIED). Only `owner_admin` sets it, with MFA and an evidence reference, and imports never overwrite it. All ten sources are UNVERIFIED, so nothing derived can be published until the owner decides. Drafting and review proceed. *Evidence:* `apps/api/tests/test_content_workflow.py` (11 tests). Bodies are technical fixtures, not academic content. | Engineering (roadmap P06, §5.4, §5.7; owner scope rule 2026-10-07) | Studio UI, import batches, preview surfaces, releases/rollback and export follow. Questions and rubrics reuse these rules in their own modules. |
| IMPL-13 | 2026-10-07 | **Native session handling.** The app exchanges an IdP access token for a `native` app session (60 days / 30 idle, IMPL-12). The session token is stored only through `expo-secure-store` (Android Keystore / iOS Keychain, `WHEN_UNLOCKED_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`), never in AsyncStorage or logs. At start-up the app validates it with `/v1/me`. A 401 anywhere (expired, or revoked from another device) clears it and returns to sign-in. Offline start-up keeps the token and offers a retry. Sign-out revokes the session on the server and always clears it locally, even offline. The Account tab offers sign-in (only the methods `runtime-config` advertises), profile summary, signed-in devices, revoke one, revoke others and sign-out. Onboarding is a stack screen with the same fields as web and no ID documents. The Expo **web** target stores the token in `sessionStorage` for development preview only. *Evidence (Expo web target, functional only; not native evidence):* register → native session → onboarding saved → profile shown; a reload keeps the session; revoke-others from the app kills the other session (401); revoke from another device signs the app out on next load; a wrong password shows the generic error; sign-out → server 401 and storage cleared. | Engineering (roadmap §5.3, P04.S1.T2) | Keystore behaviour on a device is unverified until B13/B07. OIDC PKCE needs B04. |
| IMPL-12 | 2026-10-07 | **App sessions on top of IdP tokens.** `POST /v1/sessions` accepts only a verified IdP access token (never another session) and returns an opaque `pls_` token. Only its SHA-256 is stored (`user_sessions`). Lifetimes: web 30 days absolute / 7 days idle; native 60 / 30. Users can list their sessions, revoke one (someone else's session ID → 404), revoke all others, or sign out. Revoked, expired or idle sessions → 401. **Web is a backend-for-frontend:** the token lives only in an HTTP-only `portal_session` cookie (SameSite=Lax, `Secure` in staging/production) and Next.js server code forwards it as a Bearer token; browser JavaScript never sees it. Mutations are Server Actions (POST only, Origin/Host checked by Next.js), and `next` return paths accept only same-site relative paths. Protected pages (`/account`, `/onboarding`) validate the session with the API and redirect to `/signin?next=…`. The header shows Sign in/Account from cookie presence only, so it never waits on the API. Onboarding never asks for CNIC or ID documents. *Evidence:* `apps/api/tests/test_sessions.py`; `apps/web/e2e/auth.spec.ts` (register → onboarding → profile → sessions → sign-out, protected redirects, wrong password, cross-device revoke, open-redirect refusal), 88/88 runs with `--repeat-each=4`. | Engineering (roadmap §5, P04.S1.T2/P04.S3.T1) | Web OIDC redirect (PKCE) and native secure storage need the B04 tenant; the dev adapter covers development and test only. |
